ACT 16:27 27 And the jailor, being roused out of sleep and seeing the prison doors open, drew his sword and was about to kill himself, supposing that the prisoners had escaped.
ACT 16:28 28 But Paul cried with a loud voice, saying, Do thyself no harm: for we are all here.
ACT 16:29 29 And he called for lights and sprang in, and, trembling for fear, fell down before Paul and Silas,
ACT 16:30 30 and brought them out and said, Sirs, what must I do to be saved?
ACT 16:31 31 And they said, Believe on the Lord Jesus, and thou shalt be saved, thou and thy house.
ACT 16:32 32 And they spake the word of the Lord unto him, with all that were in his house.
ACT 16:33 33 And he took them the same hour of the night, and washed their stripes; and was baptized, he and all his, immediately.
ACT 16:34 34 And he brought them up into his house, and set food before them, and rejoiced greatly, with all his house, having believed in God.
ACT 16:35 35 But when it was day, the magistrates sent the serjeants, saying, Let those men go.
ACT 16:36 36 And the jailor reported the words to Paul, saying , The magistrates have sent to let you go: now therefore come forth, and go in peace.
ACT 16:37 37 But Paul said unto them, They have beaten us publicly, uncondemned, men that are Romans, and have cast us into prison; and do they now cast us out privily? nay verily; but let them come themselves and bring us out.
ACT 16:38 38 And the serjeants reported these words unto the magistrates: and they feared when they heard that they were Romans;
ACT 16:39 39 and they came and besought them; and when they had brought them out, they asked them to go away from the city.
ACT 16:40 40 And they went out of the prison, and entered into the house of Lydia: and when they had seen the brethren, they comforted them, and departed.
ACT 17:1 1 Now when they had passed through Amphipolis and Apollonia, they came to Thessalonica, where was a synagogue of the Jews:
ACT 17:2 2 and Paul, as his custom was, went in unto them, and for three sabbath days reasoned with them from the scriptures,
ACT 17:3 3 opening and alleging that it behooved the Christ to suffer, and to rise again from the dead; and that this Jesus, whom, said he , I proclaim unto you, is the Christ.
ACT 17:4 4 And some of them were persuaded, and consorted with Paul and Silas; and of the devout Greeks a great multitude, and of the chief women not a few.
ACT 17:5 5 But the Jews, being moved with jealousy, took unto them certain vile fellows of the rabble, and gathering a crowd, set the city on an uproar; and assaulting the house of Jason, they sought to bring them forth to the people.
ACT 17:6 6 And when they found them not, they dragged Jason and certain brethren before the rulers of the city, crying, These that have turned the world upside down are come hither also;
ACT 17:7 7 whom Jason hath received: and these all act contrary to the decrees of Cæsar, saying that there is another king, one Jesus.
ACT 17:8 8 And they troubled the multitude and the rulers of the city, when they heard these things.
ACT 17:9 9 And when they had taken security from Jason and the rest, they let them go.
ACT 17:10 10 And the brethren immediately sent away Paul and Silas by night unto Beroea: who when they were come thither went into the synagogue of the Jews.
ACT 17:11 11 Now these were more noble than those in Thessalonica, in that they received the word with all readiness of mind, examining the scriptures daily, whether these things were so.
ACT 17:12 12 Many of them therefore believed; also of the Greek women of honorable estate, and of men, not a few.
ACT 17:13 13 But when the Jews of Thessalonica had knowledge that the word of God was proclaimed of Paul at Beroea also, they came thither likewise, stirring up and troubling the multitudes.
ACT 17:14 14 And then immediately the brethren sent forth Paul to go as far as to the sea: and Silas and Timothy abode there still.
ACT 17:15 15 But they that conducted Paul brought him as far as Athens: and receiving a commandment unto Silas and Timothy that they should come to him with all speed, they departed.
ACT 17:16 16 Now while Paul waited for them at Athens, his spirit was provoked within him as he beheld the city full of idols.
ACT 17:17 17 So he reasoned in the synagogue with the Jews and the devout persons, and in the marketplace every day with them that met him.
ACT 17:18 18 And certain also of the Epicurean and Stoic philosophers encountered him. And some said, What would this babbler say? others, He seemeth to be a setter forth of strange gods: because he preached Jesus and the resurrection.
ACT 17:19 19 And they took hold of him, and brought him unto the Areopagus, saying, May we know what this new teaching is, which is spoken by thee?
ACT 17:20 20 For thou bringest certain strange things to our ears: we would know therefore what these things mean.
ACT 17:21 21 (Now all the Athenians and the strangers sojourning there spent their time in nothing else, but either to tell or to hear some new thing.)
ACT 17:22 22 And Paul stood in the midst of the Areopagus, and said,
ACT 17:23 Ye men of Athens, in all things I perceive that ye are very religious.
ACT 17:24 23 For as I passed along, and observed the objects of your worship, I found also an altar with this inscription, TO AN UNKNOWN GOD. What therefore ye worship in ignorance, this I set forth unto you.
ACT 17:25 24 The God that made the world and all things therein, he, being Lord of heaven and earth, dwelleth not in temples made with hands;
ACT 17:26 25 neither is he served by men’s hands, as though he needed anything, seeing he himself giveth to all life, and breath, and all things;
ACT 17:27 26 and he made of one every nation of men to dwell on all the face of the earth, having determined their appointed seasons, and the bounds of their habitation;
ACT 17:28 27 that they should seek God, if haply they might feel after him and find him, though he is not far from each one of us:
ACT 17:29 28 for in him we live, and move, and have our being; as certain even of your own poets have said,
ACT 17:30 For we are also his offspring.
ACT 17:31 29 Being then the offspring of God, we ought not to think that the Godhead is like unto gold, or silver, or stone, graven by art and device of man.
ACT 17:32 30 The times of ignorance therefore God overlooked; but now he commandeth men that they should all everywhere repent:
ACT 17:33 31 inasmuch as he hath appointed a day in which he will judge the world in righteousness by the man whom he hath ordained; whereof he hath given assurance unto all men, in that he hath raised him from the dead.
ACT 17:34 32 Now when they heard of the resurrection of the dead, some mocked; but others said, We will hear thee concerning this yet again.
ACT 17:35 33 Thus Paul went out from among them.
ACT 17:36 34 But certain men clave unto him, and believed: among whom also was Dionysius the Areopagite, and a woman named Damaris, and others with them.
ACT 18:1 1 After these things he departed from Athens, and came to Corinth.
ACT 18:2 2 And he found a certain Jew named Aquila, a man of Pontus by race, lately come from Italy, with his wife Priscilla, because Claudius had commanded all the Jews to depart from Rome: and he came unto them;
ACT 18:3 3 and because he was of the same trade, he abode with them, and they wrought; for by their trade they were tentmakers.
ACT 18:4 4 And he reasoned in the synagogue every sabbath, and persuaded Jews and Greeks.
ACT 18:5 5 But when Silas and Timothy came down from Macedonia, Paul was constrained by the word, testifying to the Jews that Jesus was the Christ.
ACT 18:6 6 And when they opposed themselves and blasphemed, he shook out his raiment and said unto them, Your blood be upon your own heads; I am clean: from henceforth I will go unto the Gentiles.
ACT 18:7 7 And he departed thence, and went into the house of a certain man named Titus Justus, one that worshipped God, whose house joined hard to the synagogue.
ACT 18:8 8 And Crispus, the ruler of the synagogue, believed in the Lord with all his house; and many of the Corinthians hearing believed, and were baptized.
ACT 18:9 9 And the Lord said unto Paul in the night by a vision, Be not afraid, but speak and hold not thy peace:
ACT 18:10 10 for I am with thee, and no man shall set on thee to harm thee: for I have much people in this city.
ACT 18:11 11 And he dwelt there a year and six months, teaching the word of God among them.
ACT 18:12 12 But when Gallio was proconsul of Achaia, the Jews with one accord rose up against Paul and brought him before the judgment-seat,
ACT 18:13 13 saying, This man persuadeth men to worship God contrary to the law.
ACT 18:14 14 But when Paul was about to open his mouth, Gallio said unto the Jews, If indeed it were a matter of wrong or of wicked villany, O ye Jews, reason would that I should bear with you:
ACT 18:15 15 but if they are questions about words and names and your own law, look to it yourselves; I am not minded to be a judge of these matters.
ACT 18:16 16 And he drove them from the judgment-seat.
ACT 18:17 17 And they all laid hold on Sosthenes, the ruler of the synagogue, and beat him before the judgment-seat. And Gallio cared for none of these things.
ACT 18:18 18 And Paul, having tarried after this yet many days, took his leave of the brethren, and sailed thence for Syria, and with him Priscilla and Aquila: having shorn his head in Cenchreæ; for he had a vow.
ACT 18:19 19 And they came to Ephesus, and he left them there: but he himself entered into the synagogue, and reasoned with the Jews.
ACT 18:20 20 And when they asked him to abide a longer time, he consented not;
ACT 18:21 21 but taking his leave of them, and saying, I will return again unto you if God will, he set sail from Ephesus.
ACT 18:22 22 And when he had landed at Cæsarea, he went up and saluted the church, and went down to Antioch.
ACT 18:23 23 And having spent some time there , he departed, and went through the region of Galatia, and Phrygia, in order, establishing all the disciples.
ACT 18:24 24 Now a certain Jew named Apollos, an Alexandrian by race, an eloquent man, came to Ephesus; and he was mighty in the scriptures.
ACT 18:25 25 This man had been instructed in the way of the Lord; and being fervent in spirit, he spake and taught accurately the things concerning Jesus, knowing only the baptism of John:
ACT 18:26 26 and he began to speak boldly in the synagogue. But when Priscilla and Aquila heard him, they took him unto them, and expounded unto him the way of God more accurately.
ACT 18:27 27 And when he was minded to pass over into Achaia, the brethren encouraged him, and wrote to the disciples to receive him: and when he was come, he helped them much that had believed through grace;
ACT 18:28 28 for he powerfully confuted the Jews, and that publicly, showing by the scriptures that Jesus was the Christ.
ACT 19:1 1 And it came to pass, that, while Apollos was at Corinth, Paul having passed through the upper country came to Ephesus, and found certain disciples:
ACT 19:2 2 and he said unto them, Did ye receive the Holy Spirit when ye believed? And they said unto him, Nay, we did not so much as hear whether the Holy Spirit was given .
ACT 19:3 3 And he said, Into what then were ye baptized? And they said, Into John’s baptism.
ACT 19:4 4 And Paul said, John baptized with the baptism of repentance, saying unto the people that they should believe on him that should come after him, that is, on Jesus.
ACT 19:5 5 And when they heard this, they were baptized into the name of the Lord Jesus.
ACT 19:6 6 And when Paul had laid his hands upon them, the Holy Spirit came on them; and they spake with tongues, and prophesied.
ACT 19:7 7 And they were in all about twelve men.
ACT 19:8 8 And he entered into the synagogue, and spake boldly for the space of three months, reasoning and persuading as to the things concerning the kingdom of God.
ACT 19:9 9 But when some were hardened and disobedient, speaking evil of the Way before the multitude, he departed from them, and separated the disciples, reasoning daily in the school of Tyrannus.
ACT 19:10 10 And this continued for the space of two years; so that all they that dwelt in Asia heard the word of the Lord, both Jews and Greeks.
ACT 19:11 11 And God wrought special miracles by the hands of Paul:
ACT 19:12 12 insomuch that unto the sick were carried away from his body handkerchiefs or aprons, and the diseases departed from them, and the evil spirits went out.
ACT 19:13 13 But certain also of the strolling Jews, exorcists, took upon them to name over them that had the evil spirits the name of the Lord Jesus, saying, I adjure you by Jesus whom Paul preacheth.
ACT 19:14 14 And there were seven sons of one Sceva, a Jew, a chief priest, who did this.
ACT 19:15 15 And the evil spirit answered and said unto them, Jesus I know, and Paul I know; but who are ye?
ACT 19:16 16 And the man in whom the evil spirit was leaped on them, and mastered both of them, and prevailed against them, so that they fled out of that house naked and wounded.
ACT 19:17 17 And this became known to all, both Jews and Greeks, that dwelt at Ephesus; and fear fell upon them all, and the name of the Lord Jesus was magnified.
ACT 19:18 18 Many also of them that had believed came, confessing, and declaring their deeds.
ACT 19:19 19 And not a few of them that practised magical arts brought their books together and burned them in the sight of all; and they counted the price of them, and found it fifty thousand pieces of silver.
ACT 19:20 20 So mightily grew the word of the Lord and prevailed.
ACT 19:21 21 Now after these things were ended, Paul purposed in the spirit, when he had passed through Macedonia and Achaia, to go to Jerusalem, saying, After I have been there, I must also see Rome.
ACT 19:22 22 And having sent into Macedonia two of them that ministered unto him, Timothy and Erastus, he himself stayed in Asia for a while.
ACT 19:23 23 And about that time there arose no small stir concerning the Way.
ACT 19:24 24 For a certain man named Demetrius, a silversmith, who made silver shrines of Diana, brought no little business unto the craftsmen;
ACT 19:25 25 whom he gathered together, with the workmen of like occupation, and said, Sirs, ye know that by this business we have our wealth.
ACT 19:26 26 And ye see and hear, that not alone at Ephesus, but almost throughout all Asia, this Paul hath persuaded and turned away much people, saying that they are no gods, that are made with hands:
ACT 19:27 27 and not only is there danger that this our trade come into disrepute; but also that the temple of the great goddess Diana be made of no account, and that she should even be deposed from her magnificence whom all Asia and the world worshippeth.
ACT 19:28 28 And when they heard this they were filled with wrath, and cried out, saying, Great is Diana of the Ephesians.
ACT 19:29 29 And the city was filled with the confusion: and they rushed with one accord into the theatre, having seized Gaius and Aristarchus, men of Macedonia, Paul’s companions in travel.
ACT 19:30 30 And when Paul was minded to enter in unto the people, the disciples suffered him not.
ACT 19:31 31 And certain also of the Asiarchs, being his friends, sent unto him and besought him not to adventure himself into the theatre.
ACT 19:32 32 Some therefore cried one thing, and some another: for the assembly was in confusion; and the more part knew not wherefore they were come together.
ACT 19:33 33 And they brought Alexander out of the multitude, the Jews putting him forward. And Alexander beckoned with the hand, and would have made a defence unto the people.
ACT 19:34 34 But when they perceived that he was a Jew, all with one voice about the space of two hours cried out, Great is Diana of the Ephesians.
ACT 19:35 35 And when the townclerk had quieted the multitude, he saith, Ye men of Ephesus, what man is there who knoweth not that the city of the Ephesians is temple-keeper of the great Diana, and of the image which fell down from Jupiter?
ACT 19:36 36 Seeing then that these things cannot be gainsaid, ye ought to be quiet, and to do nothing rash.
ACT 19:37 37 For ye have brought hither these men, who are neither robbers of temples nor blasphemers of our goddess.
ACT 19:38 38 If therefore Demetrius, and the craftsmen that are with him, have a matter against any man, the courts are open, and there are proconsuls: let them accuse one another.
ACT 19:39 39 But if ye seek anything about other matters, it shall be settled in the regular assembly.
ACT 19:40 40 For indeed we are in danger to be accused concerning this day’s riot, there being no cause for it : and as touching it we shall not be able to give account of this concourse.
ACT 19:41 41 And when he had thus spoken, he dismissed the assembly.
ACT 20:1 1 And after the uproar ceased, Paul having sent for the disciples and exhorted them, took leave of them, and departed to go into Macedonia.
ACT 20:2 2 And when he had gone through those parts, and had given them much exhortation, he came into Greece.
ACT 20:3 3 And when he had spent three months there , and a plot was laid against him by the Jews as he was about to set sail for Syria, he determined to return through Macedonia.
ACT 20:4 4 And there accompanied him as far as Asia, Sopater of Beroea, the son of Pyrrhus; and of the Thessalonians, Aristarchus and Secundus; and Gaius of Derbe, and Timothy; and of Asia, Tychicus and Trophimus.
ACT 20:5 5 But these had gone before, and were waiting for us at Troas.
ACT 20:6 6 And we sailed away from Philippi after the days of unleavened bread, and came unto them to Troas in five days; where we tarried seven days.
ACT 20:7 7 And upon the first day of the week, when we were gathered together to break bread, Paul discoursed with them, intending to depart on the morrow; and prolonged his speech until midnight.
ACT 20:8 8 And there were many lights in the upper chamber where we were gathered together.
ACT 20:9 9 And there sat in the window a certain young man named Eutychus, borne down with deep sleep; and as Paul discoursed yet longer, being borne down by his sleep he fell down from the third story, and was taken up dead.
ACT 20:10 10 And Paul went down, and fell on him, and embracing him said, Make ye no ado; for his life is in him.
ACT 20:11 11 And when he was gone up, and had broken the bread, and eaten, and had talked with them a long while, even till break of day, so he departed.
ACT 20:12 12 And they brought the lad alive, and were not a little comforted.
ACT 20:13 13 But we, going before to the ship, set sail for Assos, there intending to take in Paul: for so had he appointed, intending himself to go by land.
ACT 20:14 14 And when he met us at Assos, we took him in, and came to Mitylene.
ACT 20:15 15 And sailing from thence, we came the following day over against Chios; and the next day we touched at Samos; and the day after we came to Miletus.
ACT 20:16 16 For Paul had determined to sail past Ephesus, that he might not have to spend time in Asia; for he was hastening, if it were possible for him, to be at Jerusalem the day of Pentecost.
ACT 20:17 17 And from Miletus he sent to Ephesus, and called to him the elders of the church.
ACT 20:18 18 And when they were come to him, he said unto them,
ACT 20:19 Ye yourselves know, from the first day that I set foot in Asia, after what manner I was with you all the time,
ACT 20:20 19 serving the Lord with all lowliness of mind, and with tears, and with trials which befell me by the plots of the Jews;
ACT 20:21 20 how I shrank not from declaring unto you anything that was profitable, and teaching you publicly, and from house to house,
ACT 20:22 21 testifying both to Jews and to Greeks repentance toward God, and faith toward our Lord Jesus Christ.
ACT 20:23 22 And now, behold, I go bound in the spirit unto Jerusalem, not knowing the things that shall befall me there:
ACT 20:24 23 save that the Holy Spirit testifieth unto me in every city, saying that bonds and afflictions abide me.
ACT 20:25 24 But I hold not my life of any account as dear unto myself, so that I may accomplish my course, and the ministry which I received from the Lord Jesus, to testify the gospel of the grace of God.
ACT 20:26 25 And now, behold, I know that ye all, among whom I went about preaching the kingdom, shall see my face no more.
ACT 20:27 26 Wherefore I testify unto you this day, that I am pure from the blood of all men.
ACT 20:28 27 For I shrank not from declaring unto you the whole counsel of God.
ACT 20:29 28 Take heed unto yourselves, and to all the flock, in which the Holy Spirit hath made you bishops, to feed the church of the Lord which he purchased with his own blood.
ACT 20:30 29 I know that after my departing grievous wolves shall enter in among you, not sparing the flock;
ACT 20:31 30 and from among your own selves shall men arise, speaking perverse things, to draw away the disciples after them.
ACT 20:32 31 Wherefore watch ye, remembering that by the space of three years I ceased not to admonish every one night and day with tears.
ACT 20:33 32 And now I commend you to God, and to the word of his grace, which is able to build you up, and to give you the inheritance among all them that are sanctified.
ACT 20:34 33 I coveted no man’s silver, or gold, or apparel.
ACT 20:35 34 Ye yourselves know that these hands ministered unto my necessities, and to them that were with me.
ACT 20:36 35 In all things I gave you an example, that so laboring ye ought to help the weak, and to remember the words of the Lord Jesus, that he himself said, It is more blessed to give than to receive.
ACT 20:37 36 And when he had thus spoken, he kneeled down and prayed with them all.
ACT 20:38 37 And they all wept sore, and fell on Paul’s neck and kissed him,
ACT 20:39 38 sorrowing most of all for the word which he had spoken, that they should behold his face no more. And they brought him on his way unto the ship.
ACT 21:1 1 And when it came to pass that we were parted from them and had set sail, we came with a straight course unto Cos, and the next day unto Rhodes, and from thence unto Patara:
ACT 21:2 2 and having found a ship crossing over unto Phoenicia, we went aboard, and set sail.
ACT 21:3 3 And when we had come in sight of Cyprus, leaving it on the left hand, we sailed unto Syria, and landed at Tyre; for there the ship was to unlade her burden.
ACT 21:4 4 And having found the disciples, we tarried there seven days: and these said to Paul through the Spirit, that he should not set foot in Jerusalem.
ACT 21:5 5 And when it came to pass that we had accomplished the days, we departed and went on our journey; and they all, with wives and children, brought us on our way till we were out of the city: and kneeling down on the beach, we prayed, and bade each other farewell;
ACT 21:6 6 and we went on board the ship, but they returned home again.
ACT 21:7 7 And when we had finished the voyage from Tyre, we arrived at Ptolemais; and we saluted the brethren, and abode with them one day.
ACT 21:8 8 And on the morrow we departed, and came unto Cæsarea: and entering into the house of Philip the evangelist, who was one of the seven, we abode with him.
ACT 21:9 9 Now this man had four virgin daughters, who prophesied.
ACT 21:10 10 And as we tarried there some days, there came down from Judæa a certain prophet, named Agabus.
ACT 21:11 11 And coming to us, and taking Paul’s girdle, he bound his own feet and hands, and said, Thus saith the Holy Spirit, So shall the Jews at Jerusalem bind the man that owneth this girdle, and shall deliver him into the hands of the Gentiles.
ACT 21:12 12 And when we heard these things, both we and they of that place besought him not to go up to Jerusalem.
ACT 21:13 13 Then Paul answered, What do ye, weeping and breaking my heart? for I am ready not to be bound only, but also to die at Jerusalem for the name of the Lord Jesus.
ACT 21:14 14 And when he would not be persuaded, we ceased, saying, The will of the Lord be done.
ACT 21:15 15 And after these days we took up our baggage and went up to Jerusalem.
ACT 21:16 16 And there went with us also certain of the disciples from Cæsarea, bringing with them one Mnason of Cyprus, an early disciple, with whom we should lodge.
ACT 21:17 17 And when we were come to Jerusalem, the brethren received us gladly.
ACT 21:18 18 And the day following Paul went in with us unto James; and all the elders were present.
ACT 21:19 19 And when he had saluted them, he rehearsed one by one the things which God had wrought among the Gentiles through his ministry.
ACT 21:20 20 And they, when they heard it, glorified God; and they said unto him, Thou seest, brother, how many thousands there are among the Jews of them that have believed; and they are all zealous for the law:
ACT 21:21 21 and they have been informed concerning thee, that thou teachest all the Jews who are among the Gentiles to forsake Moses, telling them not to circumcise their children, neither to walk after the customs.
ACT 21:22 22 What is it therefore? they will certainly hear that thou art come.
ACT 21:23 23 Do therefore this that we say to thee: We have four men that have a vow on them;
ACT 21:24 24 these take, and purify thyself with them, and be at charges for them, that they may shave their heads: and all shall know that there is no truth in the things whereof they have been informed concerning thee; but that thou thyself also walkest orderly, keeping the law.
ACT 21:25 25 But as touching the Gentiles that have believed, we wrote, giving judgment that they should keep themselves from things sacrificed to idols, and from blood, and from what is strangled, and from fornication.
ACT 21:26 26 Then Paul took the men, and the next day purifying himself with them went into the temple, declaring the fulfilment of the days of purification, until the offering was offered for every one of them.
ACT 21:27 27 And when the seven days were almost completed, the Jews from Asia, when they saw him in the temple, stirred up all the multitude and laid hands on him,
ACT 21:28 28 crying out, Men of Israel, help: This is the man that teacheth all men everywhere against the people, and the law, and this place; and moreover he brought Greeks also into the temple, and hath defiled this holy place.
ACT 21:29 29 For they had before seen with him in the city Trophimus the Ephesian, whom they supposed that Paul had brought into the temple.
ACT 21:30 30 And all the city was moved, and the people ran together; and they laid hold on Paul, and dragged him out of the temple: and straightway the doors were shut.
ACT 21:31 31 And as they were seeking to kill him, tidings came up to the chief captain of the band, that all Jerusalem was in confusion.
ACT 21:32 32 And forthwith he took soldiers and centurions, and ran down upon them: and they, when they saw the chief captain and the soldiers, left off beating Paul.
ACT 21:33 33 Then the chief captain came near, and laid hold on him, and commanded him to be bound with two chains; and inquired who he was, and what he had done.
ACT 21:34 34 And some shouted one thing, some another, among the crowd: and when he could not know the certainty for the uproar, he commanded him to be brought into the castle.
ACT 21:35 35 And when he came upon the stairs, so it was that he was borne of the soldiers for the violence of the crowd;
ACT 21:36 36 for the multitude of the people followed after, crying out, Away with him.
ACT 21:37 37 And as Paul was about to be brought into the castle, he saith unto the chief captain, May I say something unto thee? And he said, Dost thou know Greek?
ACT 21:38 38 Art thou not then the Egyptian, who before these days stirred up to sedition and led out into the wilderness the four thousand men of the Assassins?
ACT 21:39 39 But Paul said, I am a Jew, of Tarsus in Cilicia, a citizen of no mean city: and I beseech thee, give me leave to speak unto the people.
ACT 21:40 40 And when he had given him leave, Paul, standing on the stairs, beckoned with the hand unto the people; and when there was made a great silence, he spake unto them in the Hebrew language, saying,
ACT 22:1 1 Brethren and fathers, hear ye the defence which I now make unto you.
ACT 22:2 2 And when they heard that he spake unto them in the Hebrew language, they were the more quiet: and he saith,
ACT 22:3 3 I am a Jew, born in Tarsus of Cilicia, but brought up in this city, at the feet of Gamaliel, instructed according to the strict manner of the law of our fathers, being zealous for God, even as ye all are this day:
ACT 22:4 4 and I persecuted this Way unto the death, binding and delivering into prisons both men and women.
ACT 22:5 5 As also the high priest doth bear me witness, and all the estate of the elders: from whom also I received letters unto the brethren, and journeyed to Damascus to bring them also that were there unto Jerusalem in bonds to be punished.
ACT 22:6 6 And it came to pass, that, as I made my journey, and drew nigh unto Damascus, about noon, suddenly there shone from heaven a great light round about me.
ACT 22:7 7 And I fell unto the ground, and heard a voice saying unto me, Saul, Saul, why persecutest thou me?
ACT 22:8 8 And I answered, Who art thou, Lord? And he said unto me, I am Jesus of Nazareth, whom thou persecutest.
ACT 22:9 9 And they that were with me beheld indeed the light, but they heard not the voice of him that spake to me.
ACT 22:10 10 And I said, What shall I do, Lord? And the Lord said unto me, Arise, and go into Damascus; and there it shall be told thee of all things which are appointed for thee to do.
ACT 22:11 11 And when I could not see for the glory of that light, being led by the hand of them that were with me I came into Damascus.
ACT 22:12 12 And one Ananias, a devout man according to the law, well reported of by all the Jews that dwelt there,
ACT 22:13 13 came unto me, and standing by me said unto me, Brother Saul, receive thy sight. And in that very hour I looked up on him.
ACT 22:14 14 And he said, The God of our fathers hath appointed thee to know his will, and to see the Righteous One, and to hear a voice from his mouth.
ACT 22:15 15 For thou shalt be a witness for him unto all men of what thou hast seen and heard.
ACT 22:16 16 And now why tarriest thou? arise, and be baptized, and wash away thy sins, calling on his name.
ACT 22:17 17 And it came to pass, that, when I had returned to Jerusalem, and while I prayed in the temple, I fell into a trance,
ACT 22:18 18 and saw him saying unto me, Make haste, and get thee quickly out of Jerusalem; because they will not receive of thee testimony concerning me.
ACT 22:19 19 And I said, Lord, they themselves know that I imprisoned and beat in every synagogue them that believed on thee:
ACT 22:20 20 and when the blood of Stephen thy witness was shed, I also was standing by, and consenting, and keeping the garments of them that slew him.
ACT 22:21 21 And he said unto me, Depart: for I will send thee forth far hence unto the Gentiles.
ACT 22:22 22 And they gave him audience unto this word; and they lifted up their voice, and said, Away with such a fellow from the earth: for it is not fit that he should live.
ACT 22:23 23 And as they cried out, and threw off their garments, and cast dust into the air,
ACT 22:24 24 the chief captain commanded him to be brought into the castle, bidding that he should be examined by scourging, that he might know for what cause they so shouted against him.
ACT 22:25 25 And when they had tied him up with the thongs, Paul said unto the centurion that stood by, Is it lawful for you to scourge a man that is a Roman, and uncondemned?
ACT 22:26 26 And when the centurion heard it, he went to the chief captain and told him, saying, What art thou about to do? for this man is a Roman.
ACT 22:27 27 And the chief captain came and said unto him, Tell me, art thou a Roman? And he said, Yea.
ACT 22:28 28 And the chief captain answered, With a great sum obtained I this citizenship. And Paul said, But I am a Roman born.
ACT 22:29 29 They then that were about to examine him straightway departed from him: and the chief captain also was afraid when he knew that he was a Roman, and because he had bound him.
ACT 22:30 30 But on the morrow, desiring to know the certainty wherefore he was accused of the Jews, he loosed him, and commanded the chief priests and all the council to come together, and brought Paul down and set him before them.
ACT 23:1 1 And Paul, looking stedfastly on the council, said, Brethren, I have lived before God in all good conscience until this day.
ACT 23:2 2 And the high priest Ananias commanded them that stood by him to smite him on the mouth.
ACT 23:3 3 Then said Paul unto him, God shall smite thee, thou whited wall: and sittest thou to judge me according to the law, and commandest me to be smitten contrary to the law?
ACT 23:4 4 And they that stood by said, Revilest thou God’s high priest?
ACT 23:5 5 And Paul said, I knew not, brethren, that he was high priest: for it is written, Thou shalt not speak evil of a ruler of thy people.
ACT 23:6 6 But when Paul perceived that the one part were Sadducees and the other Pharisees, he cried out in the council, Brethren, I am a Pharisee, a son of Pharisees: touching the hope and resurrection of the dead I am called in question.
ACT 23:7 7 And when he had so said, there arose a dissension between the Pharisees and Sadducees; and the assembly was divided.
ACT 23:8 8 For the Sadducees say that there is no resurrection, neither angel, nor spirit; but the Pharisees confess both.
ACT 23:9 9 And there arose a great clamor: and some of the scribes of the Pharisees’ part stood up, and strove, saying, We find no evil in this man: and what if a spirit hath spoken to him, or an angel?
ACT 23:10 10 And when there arose a great dissension, the chief captain, fearing lest Paul should be torn in pieces by them, commanded the soldiers to go down and take him by force from among them, and bring him into the castle.
ACT 23:11 11 And the night following the Lord stood by him, and said, Be of good cheer: for as thou hast testified concerning me at Jerusalem, so must thou bear witness also at Rome.
ACT 23:12 12 And when it was day, the Jews banded together, and bound themselves under a curse, saying that they would neither eat nor drink till they had killed Paul.
ACT 23:13 13 And they were more than forty that made this conspiracy.
ACT 23:14 14 And they came to the chief priests and the elders, and said, We have bound ourselves under a great curse, to taste nothing until we have killed Paul.
ACT 23:15 15 Now therefore do ye with the council signify to the chief captain that he bring him down unto you, as though ye would judge of his case more exactly: and we, before he comes near, are ready to slay him.
ACT 23:16 16 But Paul’s sister’s son heard of their lying in wait, and he came and entered into the castle and told Paul.
ACT 23:17 17 And Paul called unto him one of the centurions, and said, Bring this young man unto the chief captain; for he hath something to tell him.
ACT 23:18 18 So he took him, and brought him to the chief captain, and saith, Paul the prisoner called me unto him, and asked me to bring this young man unto thee, who hath something to say to thee.
ACT 23:19 19 And the chief captain took him by the hand, and going aside asked him privately, What is it that thou hast to tell me?
ACT 23:20 20 And he said, The Jews have agreed to ask thee to bring down Paul to-morrow unto the council, as though thou wouldest inquire somewhat more exactly concerning him.
ACT 23:21 21 Do not thou therefore yield unto them: for there lie in wait for him of them more than forty men, who have bound themselves under a curse, neither to eat nor to drink till they have slain him: and now are they ready, looking for the promise from thee.
ACT 23:22 22 So the chief captain let the young man go, charging him, Tell no man that thou hast signified these things to me.
ACT 23:23 23 And he called unto him two of the centurions, and said, Make ready two hundred soldiers to go as far as Cæsarea, and horsemen threescore and ten, and spearmen two hundred, at the third hour of the night:
ACT 23:24 24 and he bade them provide beasts, that they might set Paul thereon, and bring him safe unto Felix the governor.
ACT 23:25 25 And he wrote a letter after this form:
ACT 23:26 26 Claudius Lysias unto the most excellent governor Felix, greeting.
ACT 23:27 27 This man was seized by the Jews, and was about to be slain of them, when I came upon them with the soldiers and rescued him, having learned that he was a Roman.
ACT 23:28 28 And desiring to know the cause wherefore they accused him, I brought him down unto their council:
ACT 23:29 29 whom I found to be accused about questions of their law, but to have nothing laid to his charge worthy of death or of bonds.
ACT 23:30 30 And when it was shown to me that there would be a plot against the man, I sent him to thee forthwith, charging his accusers also to speak against him before thee.
ACT 23:31 31 So the soldiers, as it was commanded them, took Paul and brought him by night to Antipatris.
ACT 23:32 32 But on the morrow they left the horsemen to go with him, and returned to the castle:
ACT 23:33 33 and they, when they came to Cæsarea and delivered the letter to the governor, presented Paul also before him.
ACT 23:34 34 And when he had read it, he asked of what province he was; and when he understood that he was of Cilicia,
ACT 23:35 35 I will hear thee fully, said he, when thine accusers also are come: and he commanded him to be kept in Herod’s palace.
ACT 24:1 1 And after five days the high priest Ananias came down with certain elders, and with an orator, one Tertullus; and they informed the governor against Paul.
ACT 24:2 2 And when he was called, Tertullus began to accuse him, saying,
ACT 24:3 Seeing that by thee we enjoy much peace, and that by thy providence evils are corrected for this nation,
ACT 24:4 3 we accept it in all ways and in all places, most excellent Felix, with all thankfulness.
ACT 24:5 4 But, that I be not further tedious unto thee, I entreat thee to hear us of thy clemency a few words.
ACT 24:6 5 For we have found this man a pestilent fellow, and a mover of insurrections among all the Jews throughout the world, and a ringleader of the sect of the Nazarenes:
ACT 24:7 6 who moreover assayed to profane the temple: on whom also we laid hold:
ACT 24:8 7
ACT 24:9 8 from whom thou wilt be able, by examining him thyself, to take knowledge of all these things whereof we accuse him.
ACT 24:10 9 And the Jews also joined in the charge, affirming that these things were so.
ACT 24:11 10 And when the governor had beckoned unto him to speak, Paul answered,
ACT 24:12 Forasmuch as I know that thou hast been of many years a judge unto this nation, I cheerfully make my defence:
ACT 24:13 11 seeing that thou canst take knowledge that it is not more than twelve days since I went up to worship at Jerusalem:
ACT 24:14 12 and neither in the temple did they find me disputing with any man or stirring up a crowd, nor in the synagogues, nor in the city.
ACT 24:15 13 Neither can they prove to thee the things whereof they now accuse me.
ACT 24:16 14 But this I confess unto thee, that after the Way which they call a sect, so serve I the God of our fathers, believing all things which are according to the law, and which are written in the prophets;
ACT 24:17 15 having hope toward God, which these also themselves look for, that there shall be a resurrection both of the just and unjust.
ACT 24:18 16 Herein I also exercise myself to have a conscience void of offence toward God and men always.
ACT 24:19 17 Now after some years I came to bring alms to my nation, and offerings:
ACT 24:20 18 amidst which they found me purified in the temple, with no crowd, nor yet with tumult: but there were certain Jews from Asia—
ACT 24:21 19 who ought to have been here before thee, and to make accusation, if they had aught against me.
ACT 24:22 20 Or else let these men themselves say what wrong-doing they found when I stood before the council,
ACT 24:23 21 except it be for this one voice, that I cried standing among them, Touching the resurrection of the dead I am called in question before you this day.
ACT 24:24 22 But Felix, having more exact knowledge concerning the Way, deferred them, saying, When Lysias the chief captain shall come down, I will determine your matter.
ACT 24:25 23 And he gave order to the centurion that he should be kept in charge, and should have indulgence; and not to forbid any of his friends to minister unto him.
ACT 24:26 24 But after certain days, Felix came with Drusilla, his wife, who was a Jewess, and sent for Paul, and heard him concerning the faith in Christ Jesus.
ACT 24:27 25 And as he reasoned of righteousness, and self-control, and the judgment to come, Felix was terrified, and answered, Go thy way for this time; and when I have a convenient season, I will call thee unto me.
ACT 24:28 26 He hoped withal that money would be given him of Paul: wherefore also he sent for him the oftener, and communed with him.
ACT 24:29 27 But when two years were fulfilled, Felix was succeeded by Porcius Festus; and desiring to gain favor with the Jews, Felix left Paul in bonds.
ACT 25:1 1 Festus therefore, having come into the province, after three days went up to Jerusalem from Cæsarea.
ACT 25:2 2 And the chief priests and the principal men of the Jews informed him against Paul; and they besought him,
ACT 25:3 3 asking a favor against him, that he would send for him to Jerusalem; laying a plot to kill him on the way.
ACT 25:4 4 Howbeit Festus answered, that Paul was kept in charge at Cæsarea, and that he himself was about to depart thither shortly.
ACT 25:5 5 Let them therefore, saith he, that are of power among you go down with me, and if there is anything amiss in the man, let them accuse him.
ACT 25:6 6 And when he had tarried among them not more than eight or ten days, he went down unto Cæsarea; and on the morrow he sat on the judgment-seat, and commanded Paul to be brought.
ACT 25:7 7 And when he was come, the Jews that had come down from Jerusalem stood round about him, bringing against him many and grievous charges which they could not prove;
ACT 25:8 8 while Paul said in his defence, Neither against the law of the Jews, nor against the temple, nor against Cæsar, have I sinned at all.
ACT 25:9 9 But Festus, desiring to gain favor with the Jews, answered Paul and said, Wilt thou go up to Jerusalem, and there be judged of these things before me?
ACT 25:10 10 But Paul said, I am standing before Cæsar’s judgment-seat, where I ought to be judged: to the Jews have I done no wrong, as thou also very well knowest.
ACT 25:11 11 If then I am a wrong-doer, and have committed anything worthy of death, I refuse not to die; but if none of those things is true whereof these accuse me, no man can give me up unto them. I appeal unto Cæsar.
ACT 25:12 12 Then Festus, when he had conferred with the council, answered, Thou hast appealed unto Cæsar: unto Cæsar shalt thou go.
ACT 25:13 13 Now when certain days were passed, Agrippa the king and Bernice arrived at Cæsarea, and saluted Festus.
ACT 25:14 14 And as they tarried there many days, Festus laid Paul’s case before the king, saying, There is a certain man left a prisoner by Felix;
ACT 25:15 15 about whom, when I was at Jerusalem, the chief priests and the elders of the Jews informed me , asking for sentence against him.
ACT 25:16 16 To whom I answered, that it is not the custom of the Romans to give up any man, before that the accused have the accusers face to face, and have had opportunity to make his defence concerning the matter laid against him.
ACT 25:17 17 When therefore they were come together here, I made no delay, but on the next day sat on the judgment-seat, and commanded the man to be brought.
ACT 25:18 18 Concerning whom, when the accusers stood up, they brought no charge of such evil things as I supposed;
ACT 25:19 19 but had certain questions against him of their own religion, and of one Jesus, who was dead, whom Paul affirmed to be alive.
ACT 25:20 20 And I, being perplexed how to inquire concerning these things, asked whether he would go to Jerusalem and there be judged of these matters.
ACT 25:21 21 But when Paul had appealed to be kept for the decision of the emperor, I commanded him to be kept till I should send him to Cæsar.
ACT 25:22 22 And Agrippa said unto Festus, I also could wish to hear the man myself. To-morrow, saith he, thou shalt hear him.
ACT 25:23 23 So on the morrow, when Agrippa was come, and Bernice, with great pomp, and they were entered into the place of hearing with the chief captains and the principal men of the city, at the command of Festus Paul was brought in.
ACT 25:24 24 And Festus saith, King Agrippa, and all men who are here present with us, ye behold this man, about whom all the multitude of the Jews made suit to me, both at Jerusalem and here, crying that he ought not to live any longer.
ACT 25:25 25 But I found that he had committed nothing worthy of death: and as he himself appealed to the emperor I determined to send him.
ACT 25:26 26 Of whom I have no certain thing to write unto my lord. Wherefore I have brought him forth before you, and specially before thee, king Agrippa, that, after examination had, I may have somewhat to write.
ACT 25:27 27 For it seemeth to me unreasonable, in sending a prisoner, not withal to signify the charges against him.
ACT 26:1 1 And Agrippa said unto Paul, Thou art permitted to speak for thyself. Then Paul stretched forth his hand, and made his defence:
ACT 26:2 2 I think myself happy, king Agrippa, that I am to make my defence before thee this day touching all the things whereof I am accused by the Jews:
ACT 26:3 3 especially because thou art expert in all customs and questions which are among the Jews: wherefore I beseech thee to hear me patiently.
ACT 26:4 4 My manner of life then from my youth up, which was from the beginning among mine own nation and at Jerusalem, know all the Jews;
ACT 26:5 5 having knowledge of me from the first, if they be willing to testify, that after the straitest sect of our religion I lived a Pharisee.
ACT 26:6 6 And now I stand here to be judged for the hope of the promise made of God unto our fathers;
ACT 26:7 7 unto which promise our twelve tribes, earnestly serving God night and day, hope to attain. And concerning this hope I am accused by the Jews, O king!
ACT 26:8 8 Why is it judged incredible with you, if God doth raise the dead?
ACT 26:9 9 I verily thought with myself that I ought to do many things contrary to the name of Jesus of Nazareth.
ACT 26:10 10 And this I also did in Jerusalem: and I both shut up many of the saints in prisons, having received authority from the chief priests, and when they were put to death I gave my vote against them.
ACT 26:11 11 And punishing them oftentimes in all the synagogues, I strove to make them blaspheme; and being exceedingly mad against them, I persecuted them even unto foreign cities.
ACT 26:12 12 Whereupon as I journeyed to Damascus with the authority and commission of the chief priests,
ACT 26:13 13 at midday, O king, I saw on the way a light from heaven, above the brightness of the sun, shining round about me and them that journeyed with me.
ACT 26:14 14 And when we were all fallen to the earth, I heard a voice saying unto me in the Hebrew language, Saul, Saul, why persecutest thou me? it is hard for thee to kick against the goad.
ACT 26:15 15 And I said, Who art thou, Lord? And the Lord said, I am Jesus whom thou persecutest.
ACT 26:16 16 But arise, and stand upon thy feet: for to this end have I appeared unto thee, to appoint thee a minister and a witness both of the things wherein thou hast seen me, and of the things wherein I will appear unto thee;
ACT 26:17 17 delivering thee from the people, and from the Gentiles, unto whom I send thee,
ACT 26:18 18 to open their eyes, that they may turn from darkness to light and from the power of Satan unto God, that they may receive remission of sins and an inheritance among them that are sanctified by faith in me.
ACT 26:19 19 Wherefore, O king Agrippa, I was not disobedient unto the heavenly vision:
ACT 26:20 20 but declared both to them of Damascus first, and at Jerusalem, and throughout all the country of Judæa, and also to the Gentiles, that they should repent and turn to God, doing works worthy of repentance.
ACT 26:21 21 For this cause the Jews seized me in the temple, and assayed to kill me.
ACT 26:22 22 Having therefore obtained the help that is from God, I stand unto this day testifying both to small and great, saying nothing but what the prophets and Moses did say should come;
ACT 26:23 23 how that the Christ must suffer, and how that he first by the resurrection of the dead should proclaim light both to the people and to the Gentiles.
ACT 26:24 24 And as he thus made his defence, Festus saith with a loud voice, Paul, thou art mad; thy much learning is turning thee mad.
ACT 26:25 25 But Paul saith, I am not mad, most excellent Festus; but speak forth words of truth and soberness.
ACT 26:26 26 For the king knoweth of these things, unto whom also I speak freely: for I am persuaded that none of these things is hidden from him; for this hath not been done in a corner.
ACT 26:27 27 King Agrippa, believest thou the prophets? I know that thou believest.
ACT 26:28 28 And Agrippa said unto Paul, With but little persuasion thou wouldest fain make me a Christian.
ACT 26:29 29 And Paul said , I would to God, that whether with little or with much, not thou only, but also all that hear me this day, might become such as I am, except these bonds.
ACT 26:30 30 And the king rose up, and the governor, and Bernice, and they that sat with them:
ACT 26:31 31 and when they had withdrawn, they spake one to another, saying, This man doeth nothing worthy of death or of bonds.
ACT 26:32 32 And Agrippa said unto Festus, This man might have been set at liberty, if he had not appealed unto Cæsar.
ACT 27:1 1 And when it was determined that we should sail for Italy, they delivered Paul and certain other prisoners to a centurion named Julius, of the Augustan band.
ACT 27:2 2 And embarking in a ship of Adramyttium, which was about to sail unto the places on the coast of Asia, we put to sea, Aristarchus, a Macedonian of Thessalonica, being with us.
ACT 27:3 3 And the next day we touched at Sidon: and Julius treated Paul kindly, and gave him leave to go unto his friends and refresh himself.
ACT 27:4 4 And putting to sea from thence, we sailed under the lee of Cyprus, because the winds were contrary.
ACT 27:5 5 And when we had sailed across the sea which is off Cilicia and Pamphylia, we came to Myra, a city of Lycia.
ACT 27:6 6 And there the centurion found a ship of Alexandria sailing for Italy; and he put us therein.
ACT 27:7 7 And when we had sailed slowly many days, and were come with difficulty over against Cnidus, the wind not further suffering us, we sailed under the lee of Crete, over against Salmone;
ACT 27:8 8 and with difficulty coasting along it we came unto a certain place called Fair Havens; nigh whereunto was the city of Lasea.
ACT 27:9 9 And when much time was spent, and the voyage was now dangerous, because the Fast was now already gone by, Paul admonished them,
ACT 27:10 10 and said unto them, Sirs, I perceive that the voyage will be with injury and much loss, not only of the lading and the ship, but also of our lives.
ACT 27:11 11 But the centurion gave more heed to the master and to the owner of the ship, than to those things which were spoken by Paul.
ACT 27:12 12 And because the haven was not commodious to winter in, the more part advised to put to sea from thence, if by any means they could reach Phoenix, and winter there; which is a haven of Crete, looking north-east and south-east.
ACT 27:13 13 And when the south wind blew softly, supposing that they had obtained their purpose, they weighed anchor and sailed along Crete, close in shore.
ACT 27:14 14 But after no long time there beat down from it a tempestuous wind, which is called Euraquilo:
ACT 27:15 15 and when the ship was caught, and could not face the wind, we gave way to it , and were driven.
ACT 27:16 16 And running under the lee of a small island called Cauda, we were able, with difficulty, to secure the boat:
ACT 27:17 17 and when they had hoisted it up, they used helps, under-girding the ship; and, fearing lest they should be cast upon the Syrtis, they lowered the gear, and so were driven.
ACT 27:18 18 And as we labored exceedingly with the storm, the next day they began to throw the freight overboard;
ACT 27:19 19 and the third day they cast out with their own hands the tackling of the ship.
ACT 27:20 20 And when neither sun nor stars shone upon us for many days, and no small tempest lay on us , all hope that we should be saved was now taken away.
ACT 27:21 21 And when they had been long without food, then Paul stood forth in the midst of them, and said, Sirs, ye should have hearkened unto me, and not have set sail from Crete, and have gotten this injury and loss.
ACT 27:22 22 And now I exhort you to be of good cheer; for there shall be no loss of life among you, but only of the ship.
ACT 27:23 23 For there stood by me this night an angel of the God whose I am, whom also I serve,
ACT 27:24 24 saying, Fear not, Paul; thou must stand before Cæsar: and lo, God hath granted thee all them that sail with thee.
ACT 27:25 25 Wherefore, sirs, be of good cheer: for I believe God, that it shall be even so as it hath been spoken unto me.
ACT 27:26 26 But we must be cast upon a certain island.
ACT 27:27 27 But when the fourteenth night was come, as we were driven to and fro in the sea of Adria, about midnight the sailors surmised that they were drawing near to some country:
ACT 27:28 28 and they sounded, and found twenty fathoms; and after a little space, they sounded again, and found fifteen fathoms.
ACT 27:29 29 And fearing lest haply we should be cast ashore on rocky ground, they let go four anchors from the stern, and wished for the day.
ACT 27:30 30 And as the sailors were seeking to flee out of the ship, and had lowered the boat into the sea, under color as though they would lay out anchors from the foreship,
ACT 27:31 31 Paul said to the centurion and to the soldiers, Except these abide in the ship, ye cannot be saved.
ACT 27:32 32 Then the soldiers cut away the ropes of the boat, and let her fall off.
ACT 27:33 33 And while the day was coming on, Paul besought them all to take some food, saying, This day is the fourteenth day that ye wait and continue fasting, having taken nothing.
ACT 27:34 34 Wherefore I beseech you to take some food: for this is for your safety: for there shall not a hair perish from the head of any of you.
ACT 27:35 35 And when he had said this, and had taken bread, he gave thanks to God in the presence of all; and he brake it, and began to eat.
ACT 27:36 36 Then were they all of good cheer, and themselves also took food.
ACT 27:37 37 And we were in all in the ship two hundred threescore and sixteen souls.
ACT 27:38 38 And when they had eaten enough, they lightened the ship, throwing out the wheat into the sea.
ACT 27:39 39 And when it was day, they knew not the land: but they perceived a certain bay with a beach, and they took counsel whether they could drive the ship upon it.
ACT 27:40 40 And casting off the anchors, they left them in the sea, at the same time loosing the bands of the rudders; and hoisting up the foresail to the wind, they made for the beach.
ACT 27:41 41 But lighting upon a place where two seas met, they ran the vessel aground; and the foreship struck and remained unmoveable, but the stern began to break up by the violence of the waves .
ACT 27:42 42 And the soldiers’ counsel was to kill the prisoners, lest any of them should swim out, and escape.
ACT 27:43 43 But the centurion, desiring to save Paul, stayed them from their purpose; and commanded that they who could swim should cast themselves overboard, and get first to the land;
ACT 27:44 44 and the rest, some on planks, and some on other things from the ship. And so it came to pass, that they all escaped safe to the land.
ACT 28:1 1 And when we were escaped, then we knew that the island was called Melita.
ACT 28:2 2 And the barbarians showed us no common kindness: for they kindled a fire, and received us all, because of the present rain, and because of the cold.
ACT 28:3 3 But when Paul had gathered a bundle of sticks and laid them on the fire, a viper came out by reason of the heat, and fastened on his hand.
ACT 28:4 4 And when the barbarians saw the venomous creature hanging from his hand, they said one to another, No doubt this man is a murderer, whom, though he hath escaped from the sea, yet Justice hath not suffered to live.
ACT 28:5 5 Howbeit he shook off the creature into the fire, and took no harm.
ACT 28:6 6 But they expected that he would have swollen, or fallen down dead suddenly: but when they were long in expectation and beheld nothing amiss come to him, they changed their minds, and said that he was a god.
ACT 28:7 7 Now in the neighborhood of that place were lands belonging to the chief man of the island, named Publius; who received us, and entertained us three days courteously.
ACT 28:8 8 And it was so, that the father of Publius lay sick of fever and dysentery: unto whom Paul entered in, and prayed, and laying his hands on him healed him.
ACT 28:9 9 And when this was done, the rest also that had diseases in the island came, and were cured:
ACT 28:10 10 who also honored us with many honors; and when we sailed, they put on board such things as we needed.
ACT 28:11 11 And after three months we set sail in a ship of Alexandria which had wintered in the island, whose sign was The Twin Brothers.
ACT 28:12 12 And touching at Syracuse, we tarried there three days.
ACT 28:13 13 And from thence we made a circuit, and arrived at Rhegium: and after one day a south wind sprang up, and on the second day we came to Puteoli;
ACT 28:14 14 where we found brethren, and were entreated to tarry with them seven days: and so we came to Rome.
ACT 28:15 15 And from thence the brethren, when they heard of us, came to meet us as far as The Market of Appius and The Three Taverns; whom when Paul saw, he thanked God, and took courage.
ACT 28:16 16 And when we entered into Rome, Paul was suffered to abide by himself with the soldier that guarded him.
ACT 28:17 17 And it came to pass, that after three days he called together those that were the chief of the Jews: and when they were come together, he said unto them, I, brethren, though I had done nothing against the people, or the customs of our fathers, yet was delivered prisoner from Jerusalem into the hands of the Romans:
ACT 28:18 18 who, when they had examined me, desired to set me at liberty, because there was no cause of death in me.
ACT 28:19 19 But when the Jews spake against it, I was constrained to appeal unto Cæsar; not that I had aught whereof to accuse my nation.
ACT 28:20 20 For this cause therefore did I entreat you to see and to speak with me : for because of the hope of Israel I am bound with this chain.
ACT 28:21 21 And they said unto him, We neither received letters from Judæa concerning thee, nor did any of the brethren come hither and report or speak any harm of thee.
ACT 28:22 22 But we desire to hear of thee what thou thinkest: for as concerning this sect, it is known to us that everywhere it is spoken against.
ACT 28:23 23 And when they had appointed him a day, they came to him into his lodging in great number; to whom he expounded the matter , testifying the kingdom of God, and persuading them concerning Jesus, both from the law of Moses and from the prophets, from morning till evening.
ACT 28:24 24 And some believed the things which were spoken, and some disbelieved.
ACT 28:25 25 And when they agreed not among themselves, they departed after that Paul had spoken one word, Well spake the Holy Spirit through Isaiah the prophet unto your fathers,
ACT 28:26 26 saying,
ACT 28:27 Go thou unto this people, and say,
ACT 28:28 By hearing ye shall hear, and shall in no wise understand;
ACT 28:29 And seeing ye shall see, and shall in no wise perceive:
ACT 28:30 27 For this people’s heart is waxed gross,
ACT 28:31 And their ears are dull of hearing,
ACT 28:32 And their eyes they have closed;
ACT 28:33 Lest haply they should perceive with their eyes,
ACT 28:34 And hear with their ears,
ACT 28:35 And understand with their heart,
ACT 28:36 And should turn again,
ACT 28:37 And I should heal them.
ACT 28:38 28 Be it known therefore unto you, that this salvation of God is sent unto the Gentiles: they will also hear.
ACT 28:39 29
ACT 28:40 30 And he abode two whole years in his own hired dwelling, and received all that went in unto him,
ACT 28:41 31 preaching the kingdom of God, and teaching the things concerning the Lord Jesus Christ with all boldness, none forbidding him.
ROM 1:1 1 Paul, a servant of Jesus Christ, called to be an apostle, separated unto the gospel of God,
ROM 1:2 2 which he promised afore through his prophets in the holy scriptures,
ROM 1:3 3 concerning his Son, who was born of the seed of David according to the flesh,
ROM 1:4 4 who was declared to be the Son of God with power, according to the spirit of holiness, by the resurrection from the dead; even Jesus Christ our Lord,
ROM 1:5 5 through whom we received grace and apostleship, unto obedience of faith among all the nations, for his name’s sake;
ROM 1:6 6 among whom are ye also, called to be Jesus Christ’s:
ROM 1:7 7 to all that are in Rome, beloved of God, called to be saints: Grace to you and peace from God our Father and the Lord Jesus Christ.
ROM 1:8 8 First, I thank my God through Jesus Christ for you all, that your faith is proclaimed throughout the whole world.
ROM 1:9 9 For God is my witness, whom I serve in my spirit in the gospel of his Son, how unceasingly I make mention of you, always in my prayers
ROM 1:10 10 making request, if by any means now at length I may be prospered by the will of God to come unto you.
ROM 1:11 11 For I long to see you, that I may impart unto you some spiritual gift, to the end ye may be established;
ROM 1:12 12 that is, that I with you may be comforted in you, each of us by the other’s faith, both yours and mine.
ROM 1:13 13 And I would not have you ignorant, brethren, that oftentimes I purposed to come unto you (and was hindered hitherto), that I might have some fruit in you also, even as in the rest of the Gentiles.
ROM 1:14 14 I am debtor both to Greeks and to Barbarians, both to the wise and to the foolish.
ROM 1:15 15 So, as much as in me is, I am ready to preach the gospel to you also that are in Rome.
ROM 1:16 16 For I am not ashamed of the gospel: for it is the power of God unto salvation to every one that believeth; to the Jew first, and also to the Greek.
ROM 1:17 17 For therein is revealed a righteousness of God from faith unto faith: as it is written, But the righteous shall live by faith.
ROM 1:18 18 For the wrath of God is revealed from heaven against all ungodliness and unrighteousness of men, who hinder the truth in unrighteousness;
ROM 1:19 19 because that which is known of God is manifest in them; for God manifested it unto them.
ROM 1:20 20 For the invisible things of him since the creation of the world are clearly seen, being perceived through the things that are made, even his everlasting power and divinity; that they may be without excuse:
ROM 1:21 21 because that, knowing God, they glorified him not as God, neither gave thanks; but became vain in their reasonings, and their senseless heart was darkened.
ROM 1:22 22 Professing themselves to be wise, they became fools,
ROM 1:23 23 and changed the glory of the incorruptible God for the likeness of an image of corruptible man, and of birds, and four-footed beasts, and creeping things.
ROM 1:24 24 Wherefore God gave them up in the lusts of their hearts unto uncleanness, that their bodies should be dishonored among themselves:
ROM 1:25 25 for that they exchanged the truth of God for a lie, and worshipped and served the creature rather than the Creator, who is blessed for ever. Amen.
ROM 1:26 26 For this cause God gave them up unto vile passions: for their women changed the natural use into that which is against nature:
ROM 1:27 27 and likewise also the men, leaving the natural use of the woman, burned in their lust one toward another, men with men working unseemliness, and receiving in themselves that recompense of their error which was due.
ROM 1:28 28 And even as they refused to have God in their knowledge, God gave them up unto a reprobate mind, to do those things which are not fitting;
ROM 1:29 29 being filled with all unrighteousness, wickedness, covetousness, maliciousness; full of envy, murder, strife, deceit, malignity; whisperers,
ROM 1:30 30 backbiters, hateful to God, insolent, haughty, boastful, inventors of evil things, disobedient to parents,
ROM 1:31 31 without understanding, covenant-breakers, without natural affection, unmerciful:
ROM 1:32 32 who, knowing the ordinance of God, that they that practise such things are worthy of death, not only do the same, but also consent with them that practise them.
ROM 2:1 1 Wherefore thou art without excuse, O man, whosoever thou art that judgest: for wherein thou judgest another, thou condemnest thyself; for thou that judgest dost practise the same things.
ROM 2:2 2 And we know that the judgment of God is according to truth against them that practise such things.
ROM 2:3 3 And reckonest thou this, O man, who judgest them that practise such things, and doest the same, that thou shalt escape the judgment of God?
ROM 2:4 4 Or despisest thou the riches of his goodness and forbearance and longsuffering, not knowing that the goodness of God leadeth thee to repentance?
ROM 2:5 5 but after thy hardness and impenitent heart treasurest up for thyself wrath in the day of wrath and revelation of the righteous judgment of God;
ROM 2:6 6 who will render to every man according to his works:
ROM 2:7 7 to them that by patience in well-doing seek for glory and honor and incorruption, eternal life:
ROM 2:8 8 but unto them that are factious, and obey not the truth, but obey unrighteousness, shall be wrath and indignation,
ROM 2:9 9 tribulation and anguish, upon every soul of man that worketh evil, of the Jew first, and also of the Greek;
ROM 2:10 10 but glory and honor and peace to every man that worketh good, to the Jew first, and also to the Greek:
ROM 2:11 11 for there is no respect of persons with God.
ROM 2:12 12 For as many as have sinned without the law shall also perish without the law: and as many as have sinned under the law shall be judged by the law;
ROM 2:13 13 for not the hearers of the law are just before God, but the doers of the law shall be justified;
ROM 2:14 14 (for when Gentiles that have not the law do by nature the things of the law, these, not having the law, are the law unto themselves;
ROM 2:15 15 in that they show the work of the law written in their hearts, their conscience bearing witness therewith, and their thoughts one with another accusing or else excusing them );
ROM 2:16 16 in the day when God shall judge the secrets of men, according to my gospel, by Jesus Christ.
ROM 2:17 17 But if thou bearest the name of a Jew, and restest upon the law, and gloriest in God,
ROM 2:18 18 and knowest his will, and approvest the things that are excellent, being instructed out of the law,
ROM 2:19 19 and art confident that thou thyself art a guide of the blind, a light of them that are in darkness,
ROM 2:20 20 a corrector of the foolish, a teacher of babes, having in the law the form of knowledge and of the truth;
ROM 2:21 21 thou therefore that teachest another, teachest thou not thyself? thou that preachest a man should not steal, dost thou steal?
ROM 2:22 22 thou that sayest a man should not commit adultery, dost thou commit adultery? thou that abhorrest idols, dost thou rob temples?
ROM 2:23 23 thou who gloriest in the law, through thy transgression of the law dishonorest thou God?
ROM 2:24 24 For the name of God is blasphemed among the Gentiles because of you, even as it is written.
ROM 2:25 25 For circumcision indeed profiteth, if thou be a doer of the law: but if thou be a transgressor of the law, thy circumcision is become uncircumcision.
ROM 2:26 26 If therefore the uncircumcision keep the ordinances of the law, shall not his uncircumcision be reckoned for circumcision?
ROM 2:27 27 and shall not the uncircumcision which is by nature, if it fulfil the law, judge thee, who with the letter and circumcision art a transgressor of the law?
ROM 2:28 28 For he is not a Jew who is one outwardly; neither is that circumcision which is outward in the flesh:
ROM 2:29 29 but he is a Jew who is one inwardly; and circumcision is that of the heart, in the spirit not in the letter; whose praise is not of men, but of God.
ROM 3:1 1 What advantage then hath the Jew? or what is the profit of circumcision?
ROM 3:2 2 Much every way: first of all, that they were intrusted with the oracles of God.
ROM 3:3 3 For what if some were without faith? shall their want of faith make of none effect the faithfulness of God?
ROM 3:4 4 God forbid: yea, let God be found true, but every man a liar; as it is written,
ROM 3:5 That thou mightest be justified in thy words,
ROM 3:6 And mightest prevail when thou comest into judgment.
ROM 3:7 5 But if our unrighteousness commendeth the righteousness of God, what shall we say? Is God unrighteous who visiteth with wrath? (I speak after the manner of men.)
ROM 3:8 6 God forbid: for then how shall God judge the world?
ROM 3:9 7 But if the truth of God through my lie abounded unto his glory, why am I also still judged as a sinner?
ROM 3:10 8 and why not (as we are slanderously reported, and as some affirm that we say), Let us do evil, that good may come? whose condemnation is just.
ROM 3:11 9 What then? are we better than they? No, in no wise: for we before laid to the charge both of Jews and Greeks, that they are all under sin;
ROM 3:12 10 as it is written,
ROM 3:13 There is none righteous, no, not one;
ROM 3:14 11 There is none that understandeth,
ROM 3:15 There is none that seeketh after God;
ROM 3:16 12 They have all turned aside, they are together become unprofitable;
ROM 3:17 There is none that doeth good, no, not so much as one:
ROM 3:18 13 Their throat is an open sepulchre;
ROM 3:19 With their tongues they have used deceit:
ROM 3:20 The poison of asps is under their lips:
ROM 3:21 14 Whose mouth is full of cursing and bitterness:
ROM 3:22 15 Their feet are swift to shed blood;
ROM 3:23 16 Destruction and misery are in their ways;
ROM 3:24 17 And the way of peace have they not known:
ROM 3:25 18 There is no fear of God before their eyes.
ROM 3:26 19 Now we know that what things soever the law saith, it speaketh to them that are under the law; that every mouth may be stopped, and all the world may be brought under the judgment of God:
ROM 3:27 20 because by the works of the law shall no flesh be justified in his sight; for through the law cometh the knowledge of sin.
ROM 3:28 21 But now apart from the law a righteousness of God hath been manifested, being witnessed by the law and the prophets;
ROM 3:29 22 even the righteousness of God through faith in Jesus Christ unto all them that believe; for there is no distinction;
ROM 3:30 23 for all have sinned, and fall short of the glory of God;
ROM 3:31 24 being justified freely by his grace through the redemption that is in Christ Jesus:
ROM 3:32 25 whom God set forth to be a propitiation, through faith, in his blood, to show his righteousness because of the passing over of the sins done aforetime, in the forbearance of God;
ROM 3:33 26 for the showing, I say , of his righteousness at this present season: that he might himself be just, and the justifier of him that hath faith in Jesus.
ROM 3:34 27 Where then is the glorying? It is excluded. By what manner of law? of works? Nay: but by a law of faith.
ROM 3:35 28 We reckon therefore that a man is justified by faith apart from the works of the law.
ROM 3:36 29 Or is God the God of Jews only? is he not the God of Gentiles also? Yea, of Gentiles also:
ROM 3:37 30 if so be that God is one, and he shall justify the circumcision by faith, and the uncircumcision through faith.
ROM 3:38 31 Do we then make the law of none effect through faith? God forbid: nay, we establish the law.
ROM 4:1 1 What then shall we say that Abraham, our forefather, hath found according to the flesh?
ROM 4:2 2 For if Abraham was justified by works, he hath whereof to glory; but not toward God.
ROM 4:3 3 For what saith the scripture? And Abraham believed God, and it was reckoned unto him for righteousness.
ROM 4:4 4 Now to him that worketh, the reward is not reckoned as of grace, but as of debt.
ROM 4:5 5 But to him that worketh not, but believeth on him that justifieth the ungodly, his faith is reckoned for righteousness.
ROM 4:6 6 Even as David also pronounceth blessing upon the man, unto whom God reckoneth righteousness apart from works,
ROM 4:7 7 saying ,
ROM 4:8 Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven,
ROM 4:9 And whose sins are covered.
ROM 4:10 8 Blessed is the man to whom the Lord will not reckon sin.
ROM 4:11 9 Is this blessing then pronounced upon the circumcision, or upon the uncircumcision also? for we say, To Abraham his faith was reckoned for righteousness.
ROM 4:12 10 How then was it reckoned? when he was in circumcision, or in uncircumcision? Not in circumcision, but in uncircumcision:
ROM 4:13 11 and he received the sign of circumcision, a seal of the righteousness of the faith which he had while he was in uncircumcision: that he might be the father of all them that believe, though they be in uncircumcision, that righteousness might be reckoned unto them;
ROM 4:14 12 and the father of circumcision to them who not only are of the circumcision, but who also walk in the steps of that faith of our father Abraham which he had in uncircumcision.
ROM 4:15 13 For not through the law was the promise to Abraham or to his seed that he should be heir of the world, but through the righteousness of faith.
ROM 4:16 14 For if they that are of the law are heirs, faith is made void, and the promise is made of none effect:
ROM 4:17 15 for the law worketh wrath; but where there is no law, neither is there transgression.
ROM 4:18 16 For this cause it is of faith, that it may be according to grace; to the end that the promise may be sure to all the seed; not to that only which is of the law, but to that also which is of the faith of Abraham, who is the father of us all
ROM 4:19 17 (as it is written, A father of many nations have I made thee) before him whom he believed, even God, who giveth life to the dead, and calleth the things that are not, as though they were.
ROM 4:20 18 Who in hope believed against hope, to the end that he might become a father of many nations, according to that which had been spoken, So shall thy seed be.
ROM 4:21 19 And without being weakened in faith he considered his own body now as good as dead (he being about a hundred years old), and the deadness of Sarah’s womb;
ROM 4:22 20 yet, looking unto the promise of God, he wavered not through unbelief, but waxed strong through faith, giving glory to God,
ROM 4:23 21 and being fully assured that what he had promised, he was able also to perform.
ROM 4:24 22 Wherefore also it was reckoned unto him for righteousness.
ROM 4:25 23 Now it was not written for his sake alone, that it was reckoned unto him;
ROM 4:26 24 but for our sake also, unto whom it shall be reckoned, who believe on him that raised Jesus our Lord from the dead,
ROM 4:27 25 who was delivered up for our trespasses, and was raised for our justification.
ROM 5:1 1 Being therefore justified by faith, we have peace with God through our Lord Jesus Christ;
ROM 5:2 2 through whom also we have had our access by faith into this grace wherein we stand; and we rejoice in hope of the glory of God.
ROM 5:3 3 And not only so, but we also rejoice in our tribulations: knowing that tribulation worketh stedfastness;
ROM 5:4 4 and stedfastness, approvedness; and approvedness, hope:
ROM 5:5 5 and hope putteth not to shame; because the love of God hath been shed abroad in our hearts through the Holy Spirit which was given unto us.
ROM 5:6 6 For while we were yet weak, in due season Christ died for the ungodly.
ROM 5:7 7 For scarcely for a righteous man will one die: for peradventure for the good man some one would even dare to die.
ROM 5:8 8 But God commendeth his own love toward us, in that, while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us.
ROM 5:9 9 Much more then, being now justified by his blood, shall we be saved from the wrath of God through him.
ROM 5:10 10 For if, while we were enemies, we were reconciled to God through the death of his Son, much more, being reconciled, shall we be saved by his life;
ROM 5:11 11 and not only so, but we also rejoice in God through our Lord Jesus Christ, through whom we have now received the reconciliation.
ROM 5:12 12 Therefore, as through one man sin entered into the world, and death through sin; and so death passed unto all men, for that all sinned:—
ROM 5:13 13 for until the law sin was in the world; but sin is not imputed when there is no law.
ROM 5:14 14 Nevertheless death reigned from Adam until Moses, even over them that had not sinned after the likeness of Adam’s transgression, who is a figure of him that was to come.
ROM 5:15 15 But not as the trespass, so also is the free gift. For if by the trespass of the one the many died, much more did the grace of God, and the gift by the grace of the one man, Jesus Christ, abound unto the many.
ROM 5:16 16 And not as through one that sinned, so is the gift: for the judgment came of one unto condemnation, but the free gift came of many trespasses unto justification.
ROM 5:17 17 For if, by the trespass of the one, death reigned through the one; much more shall they that receive the abundance of grace and of the gift of righteousness reign in life through the one, even Jesus Christ.
ROM 5:18 18 So then as through one trespass the judgment came unto all men to condemnation; even so through one act of righteousness the free gift came unto all men to justification of life.
ROM 5:19 19 For as through the one man’s disobedience the many were made sinners, even so through the obedience of the one shall the many be made righteous.
ROM 5:20 20 And the law came in besides, that the trespass might abound; but where sin abounded, grace did abound more exceedingly:
ROM 5:21 21 that, as sin reigned in death, even so might grace reign through righteousness unto eternal life through Jesus Christ our Lord.
ROM 6:1 1 What shall we say then? Shall we continue in sin, that grace may abound?
ROM 6:2 2 God forbid. We who died to sin, how shall we any longer live therein?
ROM 6:3 3 Or are ye ignorant that all we who were baptized into Christ Jesus were baptized into his death?
ROM 6:4 4 We were buried therefore with him through baptism into death: that like as Christ was raised from the dead through the glory of the Father, so we also might walk in newness of life.
ROM 6:5 5 For if we have become united with him in the likeness of his death, we shall be also in the likeness of his resurrection;
ROM 6:6 6 knowing this, that our old man was crucified with him , that the body of sin might be done away, that so we should no longer be in bondage to sin;
ROM 6:7 7 for he that hath died is justified from sin.
ROM 6:8 8 But if we died with Christ, we believe that we shall also live with him;
