ROM 6:9 9 knowing that Christ being raised from the dead dieth no more; death no more hath dominion over him.
ROM 6:10 10 For the death that he died, he died unto sin once: but the life that he liveth, he liveth unto God.
ROM 6:11 11 Even so reckon ye also yourselves to be dead unto sin, but alive unto God in Christ Jesus.
ROM 6:12 12 Let not sin therefore reign in your mortal body, that ye should obey the lusts thereof:
ROM 6:13 13 neither present your members unto sin as instruments of unrighteousness; but present yourselves unto God, as alive from the dead, and your members as instruments of righteousness unto God.
ROM 6:14 14 For sin shall not have dominion over you: for ye are not under law, but under grace.
ROM 6:15 15 What then? shall we sin, because we are not under law, but under grace? God forbid.
ROM 6:16 16 Know ye not, that to whom ye present yourselves as servants unto obedience, his servants ye are whom ye obey; whether of sin unto death, or of obedience unto righteousness?
ROM 6:17 17 But thanks be to God, that, whereas ye were servants of sin, ye became obedient from the heart to that form of teaching whereunto ye were delivered;
ROM 6:18 18 and being made free from sin, ye became servants of righteousness.
ROM 6:19 19 I speak after the manner of men because of the infirmity of your flesh: for as ye presented your members as servants to uncleanness and to iniquity unto iniquity, even so now present your members as servants to righteousness unto sanctification.
ROM 6:20 20 For when ye were servants of sin, ye were free in regard of righteousness.
ROM 6:21 21 What fruit then had ye at that time in the things whereof ye are now ashamed? for the end of those things is death.
ROM 6:22 22 But now being made free from sin and become servants to God, ye have your fruit unto sanctification, and the end eternal life.
ROM 6:23 23 For the wages of sin is death; but the free gift of God is eternal life in Christ Jesus our Lord.
ROM 7:1 1 Or are ye ignorant, brethren (for I speak to men who know the law), that the law hath dominion over a man for so long time as he liveth?
ROM 7:2 2 For the woman that hath a husband is bound by law to the husband while he liveth; but if the husband die, she is discharged from the law of the husband.
ROM 7:3 3 So then if, while the husband liveth, she be joined to another man, she shall be called an adulteress: but if the husband die, she is free from the law, so that she is no adulteress, though she be joined to another man.
ROM 7:4 4 Wherefore, my brethren, ye also were made dead to the law through the body of Christ; that ye should be joined to another, even to him who was raised from the dead, that we might bring forth fruit unto God.
ROM 7:5 5 For when we were in the flesh, the sinful passions, which were through the law, wrought in our members to bring forth fruit unto death.
ROM 7:6 6 But now we have been discharged from the law, having died to that wherein we were held; so that we serve in newness of the spirit, and not in oldness of the letter.
ROM 7:7 7 What shall we say then? Is the law sin? God forbid. Howbeit, I had not known sin, except through the law: for I had not known coveting, except the law had said, Thou shalt not covet:
ROM 7:8 8 but sin, finding occasion, wrought in me through the commandment all manner of coveting: for apart from the law sin is dead.
ROM 7:9 9 And I was alive apart from the law once: but when the commandment came, sin revived, and I died;
ROM 7:10 10 and the commandment, which was unto life, this I found to be unto death:
ROM 7:11 11 for sin, finding occasion, through the commandment beguiled me, and through it slew me.
ROM 7:12 12 So that the law is holy, and the commandment holy, and righteous, and good.
ROM 7:13 13 Did then that which is good become death unto me? God forbid. But sin, that it might be shown to be sin, by working death to me through that which is good;—that through the commandment sin might become exceeding sinful.
ROM 7:14 14 For we know that the law is spiritual: but I am carnal, sold under sin.
ROM 7:15 15 For that which I do I know not: for not what I would, that do I practise; but what I hate, that I do.
ROM 7:16 16 But if what I would not, that I do, I consent unto the law that it is good.
ROM 7:17 17 So now it is no more I that do it, but sin which dwelleth in me.
ROM 7:18 18 For I know that in me, that is, in my flesh, dwelleth no good thing: for to will is present with me, but to do that which is good is not.
ROM 7:19 19 For the good which I would I do not: but the evil which I would not, that I practise.
ROM 7:20 20 But if what I would not, that I do, it is no more I that do it, but sin which dwelleth in me.
ROM 7:21 21 I find then the law, that, to me who would do good, evil is present.
ROM 7:22 22 For I delight in the law of God after the inward man:
ROM 7:23 23 but I see a different law in my members, warring against the law of my mind, and bringing me into captivity under the law of sin which is in my members.
ROM 7:24 24 Wretched man that I am! who shall deliver me out of the body of this death?
ROM 7:25 25 I thank God through Jesus Christ our Lord. So then I of myself with the mind, indeed, serve the law of God; but with the flesh the law of sin.
ROM 8:1 1 There is therefore now no condemnation to them that are in Christ Jesus.
ROM 8:2 2 For the law of the Spirit of life in Christ Jesus made me free from the law of sin and of death.
ROM 8:3 3 For what the law could not do, in that it was weak through the flesh, God, sending his own Son in the likeness of sinful flesh and for sin, condemned sin in the flesh:
ROM 8:4 4 that the ordinance of the law might be fulfilled in us, who walk not after the flesh, but after the Spirit.
ROM 8:5 5 For they that are after the flesh mind the things of the flesh; but they that are after the Spirit the things of the Spirit.
ROM 8:6 6 For the mind of the flesh is death; but the mind of the Spirit is life and peace:
ROM 8:7 7 because the mind of the flesh is enmity against God; for it is not subject to the law of God, neither indeed can it be:
ROM 8:8 8 and they that are in the flesh cannot please God.
ROM 8:9 9 But ye are not in the flesh but in the Spirit, if so be that the Spirit of God dwelleth in you. But if any man hath not the Spirit of Christ, he is none of his.
ROM 8:10 10 And if Christ is in you, the body is dead because of sin; but the spirit is life because of righteousness.
ROM 8:11 11 But if the Spirit of him that raised up Jesus from the dead dwelleth in you, he that raised up Christ Jesus from the dead shall give life also to your mortal bodies through his Spirit that dwelleth in you.
ROM 8:12 12 So then, brethren, we are debtors, not to the flesh, to live after the flesh:
ROM 8:13 13 for if ye live after the flesh, ye must die; but if by the Spirit ye put to death the deeds of the body, ye shall live.
ROM 8:14 14 For as many as are led by the Spirit of God, these are sons of God.
ROM 8:15 15 For ye received not the spirit of bondage again unto fear; but ye received the spirit of adoption, whereby we cry, Abba, Father.
ROM 8:16 16 The Spirit himself beareth witness with our spirit, that we are children of God:
ROM 8:17 17 and if children, then heirs; heirs of God, and joint-heirs with Christ; if so be that we suffer with him , that we may be also glorified with him .
ROM 8:18 18 For I reckon that the sufferings of this present time are not worthy to be compared with the glory which shall be revealed to us-ward.
ROM 8:19 19 For the earnest expectation of the creation waiteth for the revealing of the sons of God.
ROM 8:20 20 For the creation was subjected to vanity, not of its own will, but by reason of him who subjected it, in hope
ROM 8:21 21 that the creation itself also shall be delivered from the bondage of corruption into the liberty of the glory of the children of God.
ROM 8:22 22 For we know that the whole creation groaneth and travaileth in pain together until now.
ROM 8:23 23 And not only so, but ourselves also, who have the first-fruits of the Spirit, even we ourselves groan within ourselves, waiting for our adoption, to wit , the redemption of our body.
ROM 8:24 24 For in hope were we saved: but hope that is seen is not hope: for who hopeth for that which he seeth?
ROM 8:25 25 But if we hope for that which we see not, then do we with patience wait for it.
ROM 8:26 26 And in like manner the Spirit also helpeth our infirmity: for we know not how to pray as we ought; but the Spirit himself maketh intercession for us with groanings which cannot be uttered;
ROM 8:27 27 and he that searcheth the hearts knoweth what is the mind of the Spirit, because he maketh intercession for the saints according to the will of God.
ROM 8:28 28 And we know that to them that love God all things work together for good, even to them that are called according to his purpose.
ROM 8:29 29 For whom he foreknew, he also foreordained to be conformed to the image of his Son, that he might be the firstborn among many brethren:
ROM 8:30 30 and whom he foreordained, them he also called: and whom he called, them he also justified: and whom he justified, them he also glorified.
ROM 8:31 31 What then shall we say to these things? If God is for us, who is against us?
ROM 8:32 32 He that spared not his own Son, but delivered him up for us all, how shall he not also with him freely give us all things?
ROM 8:33 33 Who shall lay anything to the charge of God’s elect? It is God that justifieth;
ROM 8:34 34 who is he that condemneth? It is Christ Jesus that died, yea rather, that was raised from the dead, who is at the right hand of God, who also maketh intercession for us.
ROM 8:35 35 Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? shall tribulation, or anguish, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword?
ROM 8:36 36 Even as it is written,
ROM 8:37 For thy sake we are killed all the day long;
ROM 8:38 We were accounted as sheep for the slaughter.
ROM 8:39 37 Nay, in all these things we are more than conquerors through him that loved us.
ROM 8:40 38 For I am persuaded, that neither death, nor life, nor angels, nor principalities, nor things present, nor things to come, nor powers,
ROM 8:41 39 nor height, nor depth, nor any other creature, shall be able to separate us from the love of God, which is in Christ Jesus our Lord.
ROM 9:1 1 I say the truth in Christ, I lie not, my conscience bearing witness with me in the Holy Spirit,
ROM 9:2 2 that I have great sorrow and unceasing pain in my heart.
ROM 9:3 3 For I could wish that I myself were anathema from Christ for my brethren’s sake, my kinsmen according to the flesh:
ROM 9:4 4 who are Israelites; whose is the adoption, and the glory, and the covenants, and the giving of the law, and the service of God , and the promises;
ROM 9:5 5 whose are the fathers, and of whom is Christ as concerning the flesh, who is over all, God blessed for ever. Amen.
ROM 9:6 6 But it is not as though the word of God hath come to nought. For they are not all Israel, that are of Israel:
ROM 9:7 7 neither, because they are Abraham’s seed, are they all children: but, In Isaac shall thy seed be called.
ROM 9:8 8 That is, it is not the children of the flesh that are children of God; but the children of the promise are reckoned for a seed.
ROM 9:9 9 For this is a word of promise, According to this season will I come, and Sarah shall have a son.
ROM 9:10 10 And not only so; but Rebecca also having conceived by one, even by our father Isaac—
ROM 9:11 11 for the children being not yet born, neither having done anything good or bad, that the purpose of God according to election might stand, not of works, but of him that calleth,
ROM 9:12 12 it was said unto her, The elder shall serve the younger.
ROM 9:13 13 Even as it is written, Jacob I loved, but Esau I hated.
ROM 9:14 14 What shall we say then? Is there unrighteousness with God? God forbid.
ROM 9:15 15 For he saith to Moses, I will have mercy on whom I have mercy, and I will have compassion on whom I have compassion.
ROM 9:16 16 So then it is not of him that willeth, nor of him that runneth, but of God that hath mercy.
ROM 9:17 17 For the scripture saith unto Pharaoh, For this very purpose did I raise thee up, that I might show in thee my power, and that my name might be published abroad in all the earth.
ROM 9:18 18 So then he hath mercy on whom he will, and whom he will he hardeneth.
ROM 9:19 19 Thou wilt say then unto me, Why doth he still find fault? For who withstandeth his will?
ROM 9:20 20 Nay but, O man, who art thou that repliest against God? Shall the thing formed say to him that formed it, Why didst thou make me thus?
ROM 9:21 21 Or hath not the potter a right over the clay, from the same lump to make one part a vessel unto honor, and another unto dishonor?
ROM 9:22 22 What if God, willing to show his wrath, and to make his power known, endured with much longsuffering vessels of wrath fitted unto destruction:
ROM 9:23 23 and that he might make known the riches of his glory upon vessels of mercy, which he afore prepared unto glory,
ROM 9:24 24 even us, whom he also called, not from the Jews only, but also from the Gentiles?
ROM 9:25 25 As he saith also in Hosea,
ROM 9:26 I will call that my people, which was not my people;
ROM 9:27 And her beloved, that was not beloved.
ROM 9:28 26 And it shall be, that in the place where it was said unto them, Ye are not my people,
ROM 9:29 There shall they be called sons of the living God.
ROM 9:30 27 And Isaiah crieth concerning Israel, If the number of the children of Israel be as the sand of the sea, it is the remnant that shall be saved:
ROM 9:31 28 for the Lord will execute his word upon the earth, finishing it and cutting it short.
ROM 9:32 29 And, as Isaiah hath said before,
ROM 9:33 Except the Lord of Sabaoth had left us a seed,
ROM 9:34 We had become as Sodom, and had been made like unto Gomorrah.
ROM 9:35 30 What shall we say then? That the Gentiles, who followed not after righteousness, attained to righteousness, even the righteousness which is of faith:
ROM 9:36 31 but Israel, following after a law of righteousness, did not arrive at that law.
ROM 9:37 32 Wherefore? Because they sought it not by faith, but as it were by works. They stumbled at the stone of stumbling;
ROM 9:38 33 even as it is written,
ROM 9:39 Behold, I lay in Zion a stone of stumbling and a rock of offence:
ROM 9:40 And he that believeth on him shall not be put to shame.
ROM 10:1 1 Brethren, my heart’s desire and my supplication to God is for them, that they may be saved.
ROM 10:2 2 For I bear them witness that they have a zeal for God, but not according to knowledge.
ROM 10:3 3 For being ignorant of God’s righteousness, and seeking to establish their own, they did not subject themselves to the righteousness of God.
ROM 10:4 4 For Christ is the end of the law unto righteousness to every one that believeth.
ROM 10:5 5 For Moses writeth that the man that doeth the righteousness which is of the law shall live thereby.
ROM 10:6 6 But the righteousness which is of faith saith thus, Say not in thy heart, Who shall ascend into heaven? (that is, to bring Christ down:)
ROM 10:7 7 or, Who shall descend into the abyss? (that is, to bring Christ up from the dead.)
ROM 10:8 8 But what saith it? The word is nigh thee, in thy mouth, and in thy heart: that is, the word of faith, which we preach:
ROM 10:9 9 because if thou shalt confess with thy mouth Jesus as Lord, and shalt believe in thy heart that God raised him from the dead, thou shalt be saved:
ROM 10:10 10 for with the heart man believeth unto righteousness; and with the mouth confession is made unto salvation.
ROM 10:11 11 For the scripture saith, Whosoever believeth on him shall not be put to shame.
ROM 10:12 12 For there is no distinction between Jew and Greek: for the same Lord is Lord of all, and is rich unto all that call upon him:
ROM 10:13 13 for, Whosoever shall call upon the name of the Lord shall be saved.
ROM 10:14 14 How then shall they call on him in whom they have not believed? and how shall they believe in him whom they have not heard? and how shall they hear without a preacher?
ROM 10:15 15 and how shall they preach, except they be sent? even as it is written, How beautiful are the feet of them that bring glad tidings of good things!
ROM 10:16 16 But they did not all hearken to the glad tidings. For Isaiah saith, Lord, who hath believed our report?
ROM 10:17 17 So belief cometh of hearing, and hearing by the word of Christ.
ROM 10:18 18 But I say, Did they not hear? Yea, verily,
ROM 10:19 Their sound went out into all the earth,
ROM 10:20 And their words unto the ends of the world.
ROM 10:21 19 But I say, Did Israel not know? First Moses saith,
ROM 10:22 I will provoke you to jealousy with that which is no nation,
ROM 10:23 With a nation void of understanding will I anger you.
ROM 10:24 20 And Isaiah is very bold, and saith,
ROM 10:25 I was found of them that sought me not;
ROM 10:26 I became manifest unto them that asked not of me.
ROM 10:27 21 But as to Israel he saith, All the day long did I spread out my hands unto a disobedient and gainsaying people.
ROM 11:1 1 I say then, Did God cast off his people? God forbid. For I also am an Israelite, of the seed of Abraham, of the tribe of Benjamin.
ROM 11:2 2 God did not cast off his people which he foreknew. Or know ye not what the scripture saith of Elijah? how he pleadeth with God against Israel:
ROM 11:3 3 Lord, they have killed thy prophets, they have digged down thine altars; and I am left alone, and they seek my life.
ROM 11:4 4 But what saith the answer of God unto him? I have left for myself seven thousand men, who have not bowed the knee to Baal.
ROM 11:5 5 Even so then at this present time also there is a remnant according to the election of grace.
ROM 11:6 6 But if it is by grace, it is no more of works: otherwise grace is no more grace.
ROM 11:7 7 What then? That which Israel seeketh for, that he obtained not; but the election obtained it, and the rest were hardened:
ROM 11:8 8 according as it is written, God gave them a spirit of stupor, eyes that they should not see, and ears that they should not hear, unto this very day.
ROM 11:9 9 And David saith,
ROM 11:10 Let their table be made a snare, and a trap,
ROM 11:11 And a stumblingblock, and a recompense unto them:
ROM 11:12 10 Let their eyes be darkened, that they may not see,
ROM 11:13 And bow thou down their back always.
ROM 11:14 11 I say then, Did they stumble that they might fall? God forbid: but by their fall salvation is come unto the Gentiles, to provoke them to jealousy.
ROM 11:15 12 Now if their fall is the riches of the world, and their loss the riches of the Gentiles; how much more their fulness?
ROM 11:16 13 But I speak to you that are Gentiles. Inasmuch then as I am an apostle of Gentiles, I glorify my ministry;
ROM 11:17 14 if by any means I may provoke to jealousy them that are my flesh, and may save some of them.
ROM 11:18 15 For if the casting away of them is the reconciling of the world, what shall the receiving of them be , but life from the dead?
ROM 11:19 16 And if the firstfruit is holy, so is the lump: and if the root is holy, so are the branches.
ROM 11:20 17 But if some of the branches were broken off, and thou, being a wild olive, wast grafted in among them, and didst become partaker with them of the root of the fatness of the olive tree;
ROM 11:21 18 glory not over the branches: but if thou gloriest, it is not thou that bearest the root, but the root thee.
ROM 11:22 19 Thou wilt say then, Branches were broken off, that I might be grafted in.
ROM 11:23 20 Well; by their unbelief they were broken off, and thou standest by thy faith. Be not highminded, but fear:
ROM 11:24 21 for if God spared not the natural branches, neither will he spare thee.
ROM 11:25 22 Behold then the goodness and severity of God: toward them that fell, severity; but toward thee, God’s goodness, if thou continue in his goodness: otherwise thou also shalt be cut off.
ROM 11:26 23 And they also, if they continue not in their unbelief, shall be grafted in: for God is able to graft them in again.
ROM 11:27 24 For if thou wast cut out of that which is by nature a wild olive tree, and wast grafted contrary to nature into a good olive tree; how much more shall these, which are the natural branches , be grafted into their own olive tree?
ROM 11:28 25 For I would not, brethren, have you ignorant of this mystery, lest ye be wise in your own conceits, that a hardening in part hath befallen Israel, until the fulness of the Gentiles be come in;
ROM 11:29 26 and so all Israel shall be saved: even as it is written,
ROM 11:30 There shall come out of Zion the Deliverer;
ROM 11:31 He shall turn away ungodliness from Jacob:
ROM 11:32 27 And this is my covenant unto them,
ROM 11:33 When I shall take away their sins.
ROM 11:34 28 As touching the gospel, they are enemies for your sake: but as touching the election, they are beloved for the fathers’ sake.
ROM 11:35 29 For the gifts and the calling of God are not repented of.
ROM 11:36 30 For as ye in time past were disobedient to God, but now have obtained mercy by their disobedience,
ROM 11:37 31 even so have these also now been disobedient, that by the mercy shown to you they also may now obtain mercy.
ROM 11:38 32 For God hath shut up all unto disobedience, that he might have mercy upon all.
ROM 11:39 33 O the depth of the riches both of the wisdom and the knowledge of God! how unsearchable are his judgments, and his ways past tracing out!
ROM 11:40 34 For who hath known the mind of the Lord? or who hath been his counsellor?
ROM 11:41 35 or who hath first given to him, and it shall be recompensed unto him again?
ROM 11:42 36 For of him, and through him, and unto him, are all things. To him be the glory for ever. Amen.
ROM 12:1 1 I beseech you therefore, brethren, by the mercies of God, to present your bodies a living sacrifice, holy, acceptable to God, which is your spiritual service.
ROM 12:2 2 And be not fashioned according to this world: but be ye transformed by the renewing of your mind, that ye may prove what is the good and acceptable and perfect will of God.
ROM 12:3 3 For I say, through the grace that was given me, to every man that is among you, not to think of himself more highly than he ought to think; but so to think as to think soberly, according as God hath dealt to each man a measure of faith.
ROM 12:4 4 For even as we have many members in one body, and all the members have not the same office:
ROM 12:5 5 so we, who are many, are one body in Christ, and severally members one of another.
ROM 12:6 6 And having gifts differing according to the grace that was given to us, whether prophecy, let us prophesy according to the proportion of our faith;
ROM 12:7 7 or ministry, let us give ourselves to our ministry; or he that teacheth, to his teaching;
ROM 12:8 8 or he that exhorteth, to his exhorting: he that giveth, let him do it with liberality; he that ruleth, with diligence; he that showeth mercy, with cheerfulness.
ROM 12:9 9 Let love be without hypocrisy. Abhor that which is evil; cleave to that which is good.
ROM 12:10 10 In love of the brethren be tenderly affectioned one to another; in honor preferring one another;
ROM 12:11 11 in diligence not slothful; fervent in spirit; serving the Lord;
ROM 12:12 12 rejoicing in hope; patient in tribulation; continuing stedfastly in prayer;
ROM 12:13 13 communicating to the necessities of the saints; given to hospitality.
ROM 12:14 14 Bless them that persecute you; bless, and curse not.
ROM 12:15 15 Rejoice with them that rejoice; weep with them that weep.
ROM 12:16 16 Be of the same mind one toward another. Set not your mind on high things, but condescend to things that are lowly. Be not wise in your own conceits.
ROM 12:17 17 Render to no man evil for evil. Take thought for things honorable in the sight of all men.
ROM 12:18 18 If it be possible, as much as in you lieth, be at peace with all men.
ROM 12:19 19 Avenge not yourselves, beloved, but give place unto the wrath of God : for it is written, Vengeance belongeth unto me; I will recompense, saith the Lord.
ROM 12:20 20 But if thine enemy hunger, feed him; if he thirst, give him to drink: for in so doing thou shalt heap coals of fire upon his head.
ROM 12:21 21 Be not overcome of evil, but overcome evil with good.
ROM 13:1 1 Let every soul be in subjection to the higher powers: for there is no power but of God; and the powers that be are ordained of God.
ROM 13:2 2 Therefore he that resisteth the power, withstandeth the ordinance of God: and they that withstand shall receive to themselves judgment.
ROM 13:3 3 For rulers are not a terror to the good work, but to the evil. And wouldest thou have no fear of the power? do that which is good, and thou shalt have praise from the same:
ROM 13:4 4 for he is a minister of God to thee for good. But if thou do that which is evil, be afraid; for he beareth not the sword in vain: for he is a minister of God, an avenger for wrath to him that doeth evil.
ROM 13:5 5 Wherefore ye must needs be in subjection, not only because of the wrath, but also for conscience’ sake.
ROM 13:6 6 For for this cause ye pay tribute also; for they are ministers of God’s service, attending continually upon this very thing.
ROM 13:7 7 Render to all their dues: tribute to whom tribute is due ; custom to whom custom; fear to whom fear; honor to whom honor.
ROM 13:8 8 Owe no man anything, save to love one another: for he that loveth his neighbor hath fulfilled the law.
ROM 13:9 9 For this, Thou shalt not commit adultery, Thou shalt not kill, Thou shalt not steal, Thou shalt not covet, and if there be any other commandment, it is summed up in this word, namely, Thou shalt love thy neighbor as thyself.
ROM 13:10 10 Love worketh no ill to his neighbor: love therefore is the fulfilment of the law.
ROM 13:11 11 And this, knowing the season, that already it is time for you to awake out of sleep: for now is salvation nearer to us than when we first believed.
ROM 13:12 12 The night is far spent, and the day is at hand: let us therefore cast off the works of darkness, and let us put on the armor of light.
ROM 13:13 13 Let us walk becomingly, as in the day; not in revelling and drunkenness, not in chambering and wantonness, not in strife and jealousy.
ROM 13:14 14 But put ye on the Lord Jesus Christ, and make not provision for the flesh, to fulfil the lusts thereof .
ROM 14:1 1 But him that is weak in faith receive ye, yet not for decision of scruples.
ROM 14:2 2 One man hath faith to eat all things: but he that is weak eateth herbs.
ROM 14:3 3 Let not him that eateth set at nought him that eateth not; and let not him that eateth not judge him that eateth: for God hath received him.
ROM 14:4 4 Who art thou that judgest the servant of another? to his own lord he standeth or falleth. Yea, he shall be made to stand; for the Lord hath power to make him stand.
ROM 14:5 5 One man esteemeth one day above another: another esteemeth every day alike . Let each man be fully assured in his own mind.
ROM 14:6 6 He that regardeth the day, regardeth it unto the Lord: and he that eateth, eateth unto the Lord, for he giveth God thanks; and he that eateth not, unto the Lord he eateth not, and giveth God thanks.
ROM 14:7 7 For none of us liveth to himself, and none dieth to himself.
ROM 14:8 8 For whether we live, we live unto the Lord; or whether we die, we die unto the Lord: whether we live therefore, or die, we are the Lord’s.
ROM 14:9 9 For to this end Christ died and lived again , that he might be Lord of both the dead and the living.
ROM 14:10 10 But thou, why dost thou judge thy brother? or thou again, why dost thou set at nought thy brother? for we shall all stand before the judgment-seat of God.
ROM 14:11 11 For it is written,
ROM 14:12 As I live, saith the Lord, to me every knee shall bow,
ROM 14:13 And every tongue shall confess to God.
ROM 14:14 12 So then each one of us shall give account of himself to God.
ROM 14:15 13 Let us not therefore judge one another any more: but judge ye this rather, that no man put a stumblingblock in his brother’s way, or an occasion of falling.
ROM 14:16 14 I know, and am persuaded in the Lord Jesus, that nothing is unclean of itself: save that to him who accounteth anything to be unclean, to him it is unclean.
ROM 14:17 15 For if because of meat thy brother is grieved, thou walkest no longer in love. Destroy not with thy meat him for whom Christ died.
ROM 14:18 16 Let not then your good be evil spoken of:
ROM 14:19 17 for the kingdom of God is not eating and drinking, but righteousness and peace and joy in the Holy Spirit.
ROM 14:20 18 For he that herein serveth Christ is well-pleasing to God, and approved of men.
ROM 14:21 19 So then let us follow after things which make for peace, and things whereby we may edify one another.
ROM 14:22 20 Overthrow not for meat’s sake the work of God. All things indeed are clean; howbeit it is evil for that man who eateth with offence.
ROM 14:23 21 It is good not to eat flesh, nor to drink wine, nor to do anything whereby thy brother stumbleth.
ROM 14:24 22 The faith which thou hast, have thou to thyself before God. Happy is he that judgeth not himself in that which he approveth.
ROM 14:25 23 But he that doubteth is condemned if he eat, because he eateth not of faith; and whatsoever is not of faith is sin.
ROM 15:1 1 Now we that are strong ought to bear the infirmities of the weak, and not to please ourselves.
ROM 15:2 2 Let each one of us please his neighbor for that which is good, unto edifying.
ROM 15:3 3 For Christ also pleased not himself; but, as it is written, The reproaches of them that reproached thee fell upon me.
ROM 15:4 4 For whatsoever things were written aforetime were written for our learning, that through patience and through comfort of the scriptures we might have hope.
ROM 15:5 5 Now the God of patience and of comfort grant you to be of the same mind one with another according to Christ Jesus:
ROM 15:6 6 that with one accord ye may with one mouth glorify the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ.
ROM 15:7 7 Wherefore receive ye one another, even as Christ also received you, to the glory of God.
ROM 15:8 8 For I say that Christ hath been made a minister of the circumcision for the truth of God, that he might confirm the promises given unto the fathers,
ROM 15:9 9 and that the Gentiles might glorify God for his mercy; as it is written,
ROM 15:10 Therefore will I give praise unto thee among the Gentiles,
ROM 15:11 And sing unto thy name.
ROM 15:12 10 And again he saith,
ROM 15:13 Rejoice, ye Gentiles, with his people.
ROM 15:14 11 And again,
ROM 15:15 Praise the Lord, all ye Gentiles;
ROM 15:16 And let all the peoples praise him.
ROM 15:17 12 And again, Isaiah saith,
ROM 15:18 There shall be the root of Jesse,
ROM 15:19 And he that ariseth to rule over the Gentiles;
ROM 15:20 On him shall the Gentiles hope.
ROM 15:21 13 Now the God of hope fill you with all joy and peace in believing, that ye may abound in hope, in the power of the Holy Spirit.
ROM 15:22 14 And I myself also am persuaded of you, my brethren, that ye yourselves are full of goodness, filled with all knowledge, able also to admonish one another.
ROM 15:23 15 But I write the more boldly unto you in some measure, as putting you again in remembrance, because of the grace that was given me of God,
ROM 15:24 16 that I should be a minister of Christ Jesus unto the Gentiles, ministering the gospel of God, that the offering up of the Gentiles might be made acceptable, being sanctified by the Holy Spirit.
ROM 15:25 17 I have therefore my glorying in Christ Jesus in things pertaining to God.
ROM 15:26 18 For I will not dare to speak of any things save those which Christ wrought through me, for the obedience of the Gentiles, by word and deed,
ROM 15:27 19 in the power of signs and wonders, in the power of the Holy Spirit; so that from Jerusalem, and round about even unto Illyricum, I have fully preached the gospel of Christ;
ROM 15:28 20 yea, making it my aim so to preach the gospel, not where Christ was already named, that I might not build upon another man’s foundation;
ROM 15:29 21 but, as it is written,
ROM 15:30 They shall see, to whom no tidings of him came,
ROM 15:31 And they who have not heard shall understand.
ROM 15:32 22 Wherefore also I was hindered these many times from coming to you:
ROM 15:33 23 but now, having no more any place in these regions, and having these many years a longing to come unto you,
ROM 15:34 24 whensoever I go unto Spain (for I hope to see you in my journey, and to be brought on my way thitherward by you, if first in some measure I shall have been satisfied with your company)—
ROM 15:35 25 but now, I say , I go unto Jerusalem, ministering unto the saints.
ROM 15:36 26 For it hath been the good pleasure of Macedonia and Achaia to make a certain contribution for the poor among the saints that are at Jerusalem.
ROM 15:37 27 Yea, it hath been their good pleasure; and their debtors they are. For if the Gentiles have been made partakers of their spiritual things, they owe it to them also to minister unto them in carnal things.
ROM 15:38 28 When therefore I have accomplished this, and have sealed to them this fruit, I will go on by you unto Spain.
ROM 15:39 29 And I know that, when I come unto you, I shall come in the fulness of the blessing of Christ.
ROM 15:40 30 Now I beseech you, brethren, by our Lord Jesus Christ, and by the love of the Spirit, that ye strive together with me in your prayers to God for me;
ROM 15:41 31 that I may be delivered from them that are disobedient in Judæa, and that my ministration which I have for Jerusalem may be acceptable to the saints;
ROM 15:42 32 that I may come unto you in joy through the will of God, and together with you find rest.
ROM 15:43 33 Now the God of peace be with you all. Amen.
ROM 16:1 1 I commend unto you Phoebe our sister, who is a servant of the church that is at Cenchreæ:
ROM 16:2 2 that ye receive her in the Lord, worthily of the saints, and that ye assist her in whatsoever matter she may have need of you: for she herself also hath been a helper of many, and of mine own self.
ROM 16:3 3 Salute Prisca and Aquila my fellow-workers in Christ Jesus,
ROM 16:4 4 who for my life laid down their own necks; unto whom not only I give thanks, but also all the churches of the Gentiles:
ROM 16:5 5 and salute the church that is in their house. Salute Epænetus my beloved, who is the firstfruits of Asia unto Christ.
ROM 16:6 6 Salute Mary, who bestowed much labor on you.
ROM 16:7 7 Salute Andronicus and Junias, my kinsmen, and my fellow-prisoners, who are of note among the apostles, who also have been in Christ before me.
ROM 16:8 8 Salute Ampliatus my beloved in the Lord.
ROM 16:9 9 Salute Urbanus our fellow-worker in Christ, and Stachys my beloved.
ROM 16:10 10 Salute Apelles the approved in Christ. Salute them that are of the household of Aristobulus.
ROM 16:11 11 Salute Herodion my kinsman. Salute them of the household of Narcissus, that are in the Lord.
ROM 16:12 12 Salute Tryphæna and Tryphosa, who labor in the Lord. Salute Persis the beloved, who labored much in the Lord.
ROM 16:13 13 Salute Rufus the chosen in the Lord, and his mother and mine.
ROM 16:14 14 Salute Asyncritus, Phlegon, Hermes, Patrobas, Hermas, and the brethren that are with them.
ROM 16:15 15 Salute Philologus and Julia, Nereus and his sister, and Olympas, and all the saints that are with them.
ROM 16:16 16 Salute one another with a holy kiss. All the churches of Christ salute you.
ROM 16:17 17 Now I beseech you, brethren, mark them that are causing the divisions and occasions of stumbling, contrary to the doctrine which ye learned: and turn away from them.
ROM 16:18 18 For they that are such serve not our Lord Christ, but their own belly; and by their smooth and fair speech they beguile the hearts of the innocent.
ROM 16:19 19 For your obedience is come abroad unto all men. I rejoice therefore over you: but I would have you wise unto that which is good, and simple unto that which is evil.
ROM 16:20 20 And the God of peace shall bruise Satan under your feet shortly.
ROM 16:21 The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ be with you.
ROM 16:22 21 Timothy my fellow-worker saluteth you; and Lucius and Jason and Sosipater, my kinsmen.
ROM 16:23 22 I Tertius, who write the epistle, salute you in the Lord.
ROM 16:24 23 Gaius my host, and of the whole church, saluteth you. Erastus the treasurer of the city saluteth you, and Quartus the brother.
ROM 16:25 24
ROM 16:26 25 Now to him that is able to establish you according to my gospel and the preaching of Jesus Christ, according to the revelation of the mystery which hath been kept in silence through times eternal,
ROM 16:27 26 but now is manifested, and by the scriptures of the prophets, according to the commandment of the eternal God, is made known unto all the nations unto obedience of faith:
ROM 16:28 27 to the only wise God, through Jesus Christ, to whom be the glory for ever. Amen.
2CO 1:1 1 Paul, an apostle of Christ Jesus through the will of God, and Timothy our brother, unto the church of God which is at Corinth, with all the saints that are in the whole of Achaia:
2CO 1:2 2 Grace to you and peace from God our Father and the Lord Jesus Christ.
2CO 1:3 3 Blessed be the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, the Father of mercies and God of all comfort;
2CO 1:4 4 who comforteth us in all our affliction, that we may be able to comfort them that are in any affliction, through the comfort wherewith we ourselves are comforted of God.
2CO 1:5 5 For as the sufferings of Christ abound unto us, even so our comfort also aboundeth through Christ.
2CO 1:6 6 But whether we are afflicted, it is for your comfort and salvation; or whether we are comforted, it is for your comfort, which worketh in the patient enduring of the same sufferings which we also suffer:
2CO 1:7 7 and our hope for you is stedfast; knowing that, as ye are partakers of the sufferings, so also are ye of the comfort.
2CO 1:8 8 For we would not have you ignorant, brethren, concerning our affliction which befell us in Asia, that we were weighed down exceedingly, beyond our power, insomuch that we despaired even of life:
2CO 1:9 9 yea, we ourselves have had the sentence of death within ourselves, that we should not trust in ourselves, but in God who raiseth the dead:
2CO 1:10 10 who delivered us out of so great a death, and will deliver: on whom we have set our hope that he will also still deliver us;
2CO 1:11 11 ye also helping together on our behalf by your supplication; that, for the gift bestowed upon us by means of many, thanks may be given by many persons on our behalf.
2CO 1:12 12 For our glorying is this, the testimony of our conscience, that in holiness and sincerity of God, not in fleshly wisdom but in the grace of God, we behaved ourselves in the world, and more abundantly to you-ward.
2CO 1:13 13 For we write no other things unto you, than what ye read or even acknowledge, and I hope ye will acknowledge unto the end:
2CO 1:14 14 as also ye did acknowledge us in part, that we are your glorying, even as ye also are ours, in the day of our Lord Jesus.
2CO 1:15 15 And in this confidence I was minded to come first unto you, that ye might have a second benefit;
2CO 1:16 16 and by you to pass into Macedonia, and again from Macedonia to come unto you, and of you to be set forward on my journey unto Judæa.
2CO 1:17 17 When I therefore was thus minded, did I show fickleness? or the things that I purpose, do I purpose according to the flesh, that with me there should be the yea yea and the nay nay?
2CO 1:18 18 But as God is faithful, our word toward you is not yea and nay.
2CO 1:19 19 For the Son of God, Jesus Christ, who was preached among you by us, even by me and Silvanus and Timothy, was not yea and nay, but in him is yea.
2CO 1:20 20 For how many soever be the promises of God, in him is the yea: wherefore also through him is the Amen, unto the glory of God through us.
2CO 1:21 21 Now he that establisheth us with you in Christ, and anointed us, is God;
2CO 1:22 22 who also sealed us, and gave us the earnest of the Spirit in our hearts.
2CO 1:23 23 But I call God for a witness upon my soul, that to spare you I forbare to come unto Corinth.
2CO 1:24 24 Not that we have lordship over your faith, but are helpers of your joy: for in faith ye stand fast.
2CO 2:1 1 But I determined this for myself, that I would not come again to you with sorrow.
2CO 2:2 2 For if I make you sorry, who then is he that maketh me glad but he that is made sorry by me?
2CO 2:3 3 And I wrote this very thing, lest, when I came, I should have sorrow from them of whom I ought to rejoice; having confidence in you all, that my joy is the joy of you all.
2CO 2:4 4 For out of much affliction and anguish of heart I wrote unto you with many tears; not that ye should be made sorry, but that ye might know the love which I have more abundantly unto you.
2CO 2:5 5 But if any hath caused sorrow, he hath caused sorrow, not to me, but in part (that I press not too heavily) to you all.
2CO 2:6 6 Sufficient to such a one is this punishment which was inflicted by the many;
2CO 2:7 7 so that contrariwise ye should rather forgive him and comfort him, lest by any means such a one should be swallowed up with his overmuch sorrow.
2CO 2:8 8 Wherefore I beseech you to confirm your love toward him.
2CO 2:9 9 For to this end also did I write, that I might know the proof of you, whether ye are obedient in all things.
2CO 2:10 10 But to whom ye forgive anything, I forgive also: for what I also have forgiven, if I have forgiven anything, for your sakes have I forgiven it in the presence of Christ;
2CO 2:11 11 that no advantage may be gained over us by Satan: for we are not ignorant of his devices.
2CO 2:12 12 Now when I came to Troas for the gospel of Christ, and when a door was opened unto me in the Lord,
2CO 2:13 13 I had no relief for my spirit, because I found not Titus my brother: but taking my leave of them, I went forth into Macedonia.
2CO 2:14 14 But thanks be unto God, who always leadeth us in triumph in Christ, and maketh manifest through us the savor of his knowledge in every place.
2CO 2:15 15 For we are a sweet savor of Christ unto God, in them that are saved, and in them that perish;
2CO 2:16 16 to the one a savor from death unto death; to the other a savor from life unto life. And who is sufficient for these things?
2CO 2:17 17 For we are not as the many, corrupting the word of God: but as of sincerity, but as of God, in the sight of God, speak we in Christ.
2CO 3:1 1 Are we beginning again to commend ourselves? or need we, as do some, epistles of commendation to you or from you?
2CO 3:2 2 Ye are our epistle, written in our hearts, known and read of all men;
2CO 3:3 3 being made manifest that ye are an epistle of Christ, ministered by us, written not with ink, but with the Spirit of the living God; not in tables of stone, but in tables that are hearts of flesh.
2CO 3:4 4 And such confidence have we through Christ to God-ward:
2CO 3:5 5 not that we are sufficient of ourselves, to account anything as from ourselves; but our sufficiency is from God;
2CO 3:6 6 who also made us sufficient as ministers of a new covenant; not of the letter, but of the spirit: for the letter killeth, but the spirit giveth life.
2CO 3:7 7 But if the ministration of death, written, and engraven on stones, came with glory, so that the children of Israel could not look stedfastly upon the face of Moses for the glory of his face; which glory was passing away:
2CO 3:8 8 how shall not rather the ministration of the spirit be with glory?
2CO 3:9 9 For if the ministration of condemnation hath glory, much rather doth the ministration of righteousness exceed in glory.
2CO 3:10 10 For verily that which hath been made glorious hath not been made glorious in this respect, by reason of the glory that surpasseth.
2CO 3:11 11 For if that which passeth away was with glory, much more that which remaineth is in glory.
2CO 3:12 12 Having therefore such a hope, we use great boldness of speech,
2CO 3:13 13 and are not as Moses, who put a veil upon his face, that the children of Israel should not look stedfastly on the end of that which was passing away:
2CO 3:14 14 but their minds were hardened: for until this very day at the reading of the old covenant the same veil remaineth, it not being revealed to them that it is done away in Christ.
2CO 3:15 15 But unto this day, whensoever Moses is read, a veil lieth upon their heart.
2CO 3:16 16 But whensoever it shall turn to the Lord, the veil is taken away.
2CO 3:17 17 Now the Lord is the Spirit: and where the Spirit of the Lord is, there is liberty.
2CO 3:18 18 But we all, with unveiled face beholding as in a mirror the glory of the Lord, are transformed into the same image from glory to glory, even as from the Lord the Spirit.
2CO 4:1 1 Therefore seeing we have this ministry, even as we obtained mercy, we faint not:
2CO 4:2 2 but we have renounced the hidden things of shame, not walking in craftiness, nor handling the word of God deceitfully; but by the manifestation of the truth commending ourselves to every man’s conscience in the sight of God.
2CO 4:3 3 And even if our gospel is veiled, it is veiled in them that perish:
2CO 4:4 4 in whom the god of this world hath blinded the minds of the unbelieving, that the light of the gospel of the glory of Christ, who is the image of God, should not dawn upon them .
2CO 4:5 5 For we preach not ourselves, but Christ Jesus as Lord, and ourselves as your servants for Jesus’ sake.
2CO 4:6 6 Seeing it is God, that said, Light shall shine out of darkness, who shined in our hearts, to give the light of the knowledge of the glory of God in the face of Jesus Christ.
2CO 4:7 7 But we have this treasure in earthen vessels, that the exceeding greatness of the power may be of God, and not from ourselves;
2CO 4:8 8 we are pressed on every side, yet not straitened; perplexed, yet not unto despair;
2CO 4:9 9 pursued, yet not forsaken; smitten down, yet not destroyed;
2CO 4:10 10 always bearing about in the body the dying of Jesus, that the life also of Jesus may be manifested in our body.
2CO 4:11 11 For we who live are always delivered unto death for Jesus’ sake, that the life also of Jesus may be manifested in our mortal flesh.
2CO 4:12 12 So then death worketh in us, but life in you.
2CO 4:13 13 But having the same spirit of faith, according to that which is written, I believed, and therefore did I speak; we also believe, and therefore also we speak;
2CO 4:14 14 knowing that he that raised up the Lord Jesus shall raise up us also with Jesus, and shall present us with you.
2CO 4:15 15 For all things are for your sakes, that the grace, being multiplied through the many, may cause the thanksgiving to abound unto the glory of God.
2CO 4:16 16 Wherefore we faint not; but though our outward man is decaying, yet our inward man is renewed day by day.
2CO 4:17 17 For our light affliction, which is for the moment, worketh for us more and more exceedingly an eternal weight of glory;
2CO 4:18 18 while we look not at the things which are seen, but at the things which are not seen: for the things which are seen are temporal; but the things which are not seen are eternal.
2CO 5:1 1 For we know that if the earthly house of our tabernacle be dissolved, we have a building from God, a house not made with hands, eternal, in the heavens.
2CO 5:2 2 For verily in this we groan, longing to be clothed upon with our habitation which is from heaven:
2CO 5:3 3 if so be that being clothed we shall not be found naked.
2CO 5:4 4 For indeed we that are in this tabernacle do groan, being burdened; not for that we would be unclothed, but that we would be clothed upon, that what is mortal may be swallowed up of life.
2CO 5:5 5 Now he that wrought us for this very thing is God, who gave unto us the earnest of the Spirit.
2CO 5:6 6 Being therefore always of good courage, and knowing that, whilst we are at home in the body, we are absent from the Lord
2CO 5:7 7 (for we walk by faith, not by sight);
2CO 5:8 8 we are of good courage, I say, and are willing rather to be absent from the body, and to be at home with the Lord.
2CO 5:9 9 Wherefore also we make it our aim, whether at home or absent, to be well-pleasing unto him.
2CO 5:10 10 For we must all be made manifest before the judgment-seat of Christ; that each one may receive the things done in the body, according to what he hath done, whether it be good or bad.
2CO 5:11 11 Knowing therefore the fear of the Lord, we persuade men, but we are made manifest unto God; and I hope that we are made manifest also in your consciences.
2CO 5:12 12 We are not again commending ourselves unto you, but speak as giving you occasion of glorying on our behalf, that ye may have wherewith to answer them that glory in appearance, and not in heart.
2CO 5:13 13 For whether we are beside ourselves, it is unto God; or whether we are of sober mind, it is unto you.
2CO 5:14 14 For the love of Christ constraineth us; because we thus judge, that one died for all, therefore all died;
2CO 5:15 15 and he died for all, that they that live should no longer live unto themselves, but unto him who for their sakes died and rose again.
2CO 5:16 16 Wherefore we henceforth know no man after the flesh: even though we have known Christ after the flesh, yet now we know him so no more.
2CO 5:17 17 Wherefore if any man is in Christ, he is a new creature: the old things are passed away; behold, they are become new.
2CO 5:18 18 But all things are of God, who reconciled us to himself through Christ, and gave unto us the ministry of reconciliation;
2CO 5:19 19 to wit, that God was in Christ reconciling the world unto himself, not reckoning unto them their trespasses, and having committed unto us the word of reconciliation.
2CO 5:20 20 We are ambassadors therefore on behalf of Christ, as though God were entreating by us: we beseech you on behalf of Christ, be ye reconciled to God.
2CO 5:21 21 Him who knew no sin he made to be sin on our behalf; that we might become the righteousness of God in him.
2CO 6:1 1 And working together with him we entreat also that ye receive not the grace of God in vain
2CO 6:2 2 (for he saith,
2CO 6:3 At an acceptable time I hearkened unto thee,
2CO 6:4 And in a day of salvation did I succor thee:
2CO 6:5 behold, now is the acceptable time; behold, now is the day of salvation):
2CO 6:6 3 giving no occasion of stumbling in anything, that our ministration be not blamed;
2CO 6:7 4 but in everything commending ourselves, as ministers of God, in much patience, in afflictions, in necessities, in distresses,
2CO 6:8 5 in stripes, in imprisonments, in tumults, in labors, in watchings, in fastings;
2CO 6:9 6 in pureness, in knowledge, in longsuffering, in kindness, in the Holy Spirit, in love unfeigned,
2CO 6:10 7 in the word of truth, in the power of God; by the armor of righteousness on the right hand and on the left,
2CO 6:11 8 by glory and dishonor, by evil report and good report; as deceivers, and yet true;
2CO 6:12 9 as unknown, and yet well known; as dying, and behold, we live; as chastened, and not killed;
2CO 6:13 10 as sorrowful, yet always rejoicing; as poor, yet making many rich; as having nothing, and yet possessing all things.
2CO 6:14 11 Our mouth is open unto you, O Corinthians, our heart is enlarged.
2CO 6:15 12 Ye are not straitened in us, but ye are straitened in your own affections.
2CO 6:16 13 Now for a recompense in like kind (I speak as unto my children), be ye also enlarged.
2CO 6:17 14 Be not unequally yoked with unbelievers: for what fellowship have righteousness and iniquity? or what communion hath light with darkness?
2CO 6:18 15 And what concord hath Christ with Belial? or what portion hath a believer with an unbeliever?
2CO 6:19 16 And what agreement hath a temple of God with idols? for we are a temple of the living God; even as God said, I will dwell in them, and walk in them; and I will be their God, and they shall be my people.
2CO 6:20 17 Wherefore
2CO 6:21 Come ye out from among them, and be ye separate,
2CO 6:22 saith the Lord,
2CO 6:23 And touch no unclean thing;
2CO 6:24 And I will receive you,
2CO 6:25 18 And will be to you a Father,
2CO 6:26 And ye shall be to me sons and daughters,
2CO 6:27 saith the Lord Almighty.
2CO 7:1 1 Having therefore these promises, beloved, let us cleanse ourselves from all defilement of flesh and spirit, perfecting holiness in the fear of God.
2CO 7:2 2 Open your hearts to us: we wronged no man, we corrupted no man, we took advantage of no man.
2CO 7:3 3 I say it not to condemn you : for I have said before, that ye are in our hearts to die together and live together.
2CO 7:4 4 Great is my boldness of speech toward you, great is my glorying on your behalf: I am filled with comfort, I overflow with joy in all our affliction.
2CO 7:5 5 For even when we were come into Macedonia our flesh had no relief, but we were afflicted on every side; without were fightings, within were fears.
2CO 7:6 6 Nevertheless he that comforteth the lowly, even God, comforted us by the coming of Titus;
2CO 7:7 7 and not by his coming only, but also by the comfort wherewith he was comforted in you, while he told us your longing, your mourning, your zeal for me; so that I rejoiced yet more.
2CO 7:8 8 For though I made you sorry with my epistle, I do not regret it: though I did regret it (for I see that that epistle made you sorry, though but for a season),
2CO 7:9 9 I now rejoice, not that ye were made sorry, but that ye were made sorry unto repentance; for ye were made sorry after a godly sort, that ye might suffer loss by us in nothing.
2CO 7:10 10 For godly sorrow worketh repentance unto salvation, a repentance which bringeth no regret: but the sorrow of the world worketh death.
2CO 7:11 11 For behold, this selfsame thing, that ye were made sorry after a godly sort, what earnest care it wrought in you, yea what clearing of yourselves, yea what indignation, yea what fear, yea what longing, yea what zeal, yea what avenging! In everything ye approved yourselves to be pure in the matter.
2CO 7:12 12 So although I wrote unto you, I wrote not for his cause that did the wrong, nor for his cause that suffered the wrong, but that your earnest care for us might be made manifest unto you in the sight of God.
2CO 7:13 13 Therefore we have been comforted: and in our comfort we joyed the more exceedingly for the joy of Titus, because his spirit hath been refreshed by you all.
2CO 7:14 14 For if in anything I have gloried to him on your behalf, I was not put to shame; but as we spake all things to you in truth, so our glorying also which I made before Titus was found to be truth.
2CO 7:15 15 And his affection is more abundantly toward you, while he remembereth the obedience of you all, how with fear and trembling ye received him.
2CO 7:16 16 I rejoice that in everything I am of good courage concerning you.
2CO 8:1 1 Moreover, brethren, we make known to you the grace of God which hath been given in the churches of Macedonia;
2CO 8:2 2 how that in much proof of affliction the abundance of their joy and their deep poverty abounded unto the riches of their liberality.
2CO 8:3 3 For according to their power, I bear witness, yea and beyond their power, they gave of their own accord,
2CO 8:4 4 beseeching us with much entreaty in regard of this grace and the fellowship in the ministering to the saints:
2CO 8:5 5 and this , not as we had hoped, but first they gave their own selves to the Lord, and to us through the will of God.
2CO 8:6 6 Insomuch that we exhorted Titus, that as he had made a beginning before, so he would also complete in you this grace also.
2CO 8:7 7 But as ye abound in everything, in faith, and utterance, and knowledge, and in all earnestness, and in your love to us, see that ye abound in this grace also.
2CO 8:8 8 I speak not by way of commandment, but as proving through the earnestness of others the sincerity also of your love.
2CO 8:9 9 For ye know the grace of our Lord Jesus Christ, that, though he was rich, yet for your sakes he became poor, that ye through his poverty might become rich.
2CO 8:10 10 And herein I give my judgment: for this is expedient for you, who were the first to make a beginning a year ago, not only to do, but also to will.
2CO 8:11 11 But now complete the doing also; that as there was the readiness to will, so there may be the completion also out of your ability.
2CO 8:12 12 For if the readiness is there, it is acceptable according as a man hath, not according as he hath not.
2CO 8:13 13 For I say not this that others may be eased and ye distressed;
2CO 8:14 14 but by equality: your abundance being a supply at this present time for their want, that their abundance also may become a supply for your want; that there may be equality:
2CO 8:15 15 as it is written, He that gathered much had nothing over; and he that gathered little had no lack.
2CO 8:16 16 But thanks be to God, who putteth the same earnest care for you into the heart of Titus.
2CO 8:17 17 For he accepted indeed our exhortation; but being himself very earnest, he went forth unto you of his own accord.
2CO 8:18 18 And we have sent together with him the brother whose praise in the gospel is spread through all the churches;
2CO 8:19 19 and not only so, but who was also appointed by the churches to travel with us in the matter of this grace, which is ministered by us to the glory of the Lord, and to show our readiness:
2CO 8:20 20 avoiding this, that any man should blame us in the matter of this bounty which is ministered by us:
2CO 8:21 21 for we take thought for things honorable, not only in the sight of the Lord, but also in the sight of men.
2CO 8:22 22 And we have sent with them our brother, whom we have many times proved earnest in many things, but now much more earnest, by reason of the great confidence which he hath in you.
2CO 8:23 23 Whether any inquire about Titus, he is my partner and my fellow-worker to you-ward; or our brethren, they are the messengers of the churches, they are the glory of Christ.
2CO 8:24 24 Show ye therefore unto them in the face of the churches the proof of your love, and of our glorying on your behalf.
2CO 9:1 1 For as touching the ministering to the saints, it is superfluous for me to write to you:
2CO 9:2 2 for I know your readiness, of which I glory on your behalf to them of Macedonia, that Achaia hath been prepared for a year past; and your zeal hath stirred up very many of them.
2CO 9:3 3 But I have sent the brethren, that our glorying on your behalf may not be made void in this respect; that, even as I said, ye may be prepared:
2CO 9:4 4 lest by any means, if there come with me any of Macedonia and find you unprepared, we (that we say not, ye) should be put to shame in this confidence.
2CO 9:5 5 I thought it necessary therefore to entreat the brethren, that they would go before unto you, and make up beforehand your aforepromised bounty, that the same might be ready as a matter of bounty, and not of extortion.
2CO 9:6 6 But this I say , He that soweth sparingly shall reap also sparingly; and he that soweth bountifully shall reap also bountifully.
2CO 9:7 7 Let each man do according as he hath purposed in his heart: not grudgingly, or of necessity: for God loveth a cheerful giver.
2CO 9:8 8 And God is able to make all grace abound unto you; that ye, having always all sufficiency in everything, may abound unto every good work:
2CO 9:9 9 as it is written,
2CO 9:10 He hath scattered abroad, he hath given to the poor;
2CO 9:11 His righteousness abideth for ever.
2CO 9:12 10 And he that supplieth seed to the sower and bread for food, shall supply and multiply your seed for sowing, and increase the fruits of your righteousness:
2CO 9:13 11 ye being enriched in everything unto all liberality, which worketh through us thanksgiving to God.
2CO 9:14 12 For the ministration of this service not only filleth up the measure of the wants of the saints, but aboundeth also through many thanksgivings unto God;
2CO 9:15 13 seeing that through the proving of you by this ministration they glorify God for the obedience of your confession unto the gospel of Christ, and for the liberality of your contribution unto them and unto all;
2CO 9:16 14 while they themselves also, with supplication on your behalf, long after you by reason of the exceeding grace of God in you.
2CO 9:17 15 Thanks be to God for his unspeakable gift.
2CO 10:1 1 Now I Paul myself entreat you by the meekness and gentleness of Christ, I who in your presence am lowly among you, but being absent am of good courage toward you:
2CO 10:2 2 yea, I beseech you, that I may not when present show courage with the confidence wherewith I count to be bold against some, who count of us as if we walked according to the flesh.
2CO 10:3 3 For though we walk in the flesh, we do not war according to the flesh
2CO 10:4 4 (for the weapons of our warfare are not of the flesh, but mighty before God to the casting down of strongholds);
2CO 10:5 5 casting down imaginations, and every high thing that is exalted against the knowledge of God, and bringing every thought into captivity to the obedience of Christ;
2CO 10:6 6 and being in readiness to avenge all disobedience, when your obedience shall be made full.
2CO 10:7 7 Ye look at the things that are before your face. If any man trusteth in himself that he is Christ’s, let him consider this again with himself, that, even as he is Christ’s, so also are we.
2CO 10:8 8 For though I should glory somewhat abundantly concerning our authority (which the Lord gave for building you up, and not for casting you down), I shall not be put to shame:
2CO 10:9 9 that I may not seem as if I would terrify you by my letters.
2CO 10:10 10 For, His letters, they say, are weighty and strong; but his bodily presence is weak, and his speech of no account.
2CO 10:11 11 Let such a one reckon this, that, what we are in word by letters when we are absent, such are we also in deed when we are present.
2CO 10:12 12 For we are not bold to number or compare ourselves with certain of them that commend themselves: but they themselves, measuring themselves by themselves, and comparing themselves with themselves, are without understanding.
2CO 10:13 13 But we will not glory beyond our measure, but according to the measure of the province which God apportioned to us as a measure, to reach even unto you.
2CO 10:14 14 For we stretch not ourselves overmuch, as though we reached not unto you: for we came even as far as unto you in the gospel of Christ:
2CO 10:15 15 not glorying beyond our measure, that is , in other men’s labors; but having hope that, as your faith groweth, we shall be magnified in you according to our province unto further abundance,
2CO 10:16 16 so as to preach the gospel even unto the parts beyond you, and not to glory in another’s province in regard of things ready to our hand.
2CO 10:17 17 But he that glorieth, let him glory in the Lord.
2CO 10:18 18 For not he that commendeth himself is approved, but whom the Lord commendeth.
2CO 11:1 1 Would that ye could bear with me in a little foolishness: but indeed ye do bear with me.
2CO 11:2 2 For I am jealous over you with a godly jealousy: for I espoused you to one husband, that I might present you as a pure virgin to Christ.
2CO 11:3 3 But I fear, lest by any means, as the serpent beguiled Eve in his craftiness, your minds should be corrupted from the simplicity and the purity that is toward Christ.
2CO 11:4 4 For if he that cometh preacheth another Jesus, whom we did not preach, or if ye receive a different spirit, which ye did not receive, or a different gospel, which ye did not accept, ye do well to bear with him .
2CO 11:5 5 For I reckon that I am not a whit behind the very chiefest apostles.
2CO 11:6 6 But though I be rude in speech, yet am I not in knowledge; nay, in every way have we made this manifest unto you in all things.
2CO 11:7 7 Or did I commit a sin in abasing myself that ye might be exalted, because I preached to you the gospel of God for nought?
2CO 11:8 8 I robbed other churches, taking wages of them that I might minister unto you;
2CO 11:9 9 and when I was present with you and was in want, I was not a burden on any man; for the brethren, when they came from Macedonia, supplied the measure of my want; and in everything I kept myself from being burdensome unto you, and so will I keep myself .
2CO 11:10 10 As the truth of Christ is in me, no man shall stop me of this glorying in the regions of Achaia.
2CO 11:11 11 Wherefore? because I love you not? God knoweth.
2CO 11:12 12 But what I do, that I will do, that I may cut off occasion from them that desire an occasion; that wherein they glory, they may be found even as we.
2CO 11:13 13 For such men are false apostles, deceitful workers, fashioning themselves into apostles of Christ.
2CO 11:14 14 And no marvel; for even Satan fashioneth himself into an angel of light.
2CO 11:15 15 It is no great thing therefore if his ministers also fashion themselves as ministers of righteousness; whose end shall be according to their works.
2CO 11:16 16 I say again, Let no man think me foolish; but if ye do , yet as foolish receive me, that I also may glory a little.
2CO 11:17 17 That which I speak, I speak not after the Lord, but as in foolishness, in this confidence of glorying.
2CO 11:18 18 Seeing that many glory after the flesh, I will glory also.
2CO 11:19 19 For ye bear with the foolish gladly, being wise yourselves .
2CO 11:20 20 For ye bear with a man, if he bringeth you into bondage, if he devoureth you, if he taketh you captive , if he exalteth himself, if he smiteth you on the face.
2CO 11:21 21 I speak by way of disparagement, as though we had been weak. Yet whereinsoever any is bold (I speak in foolishness), I am bold also.
2CO 11:22 22 Are they Hebrews? so am I. Are they Israelites? so am I. Are they the seed of Abraham? so am I.
2CO 11:23 23 Are they ministers of Christ? (I speak as one beside himself) I more; in labors more abundantly, in prisons more abundantly, in stripes above measure, in deaths oft.
2CO 11:24 24 Of the Jews five times received I forty stripes save one.
2CO 11:25 25 Thrice was I beaten with rods, once was I stoned, thrice I suffered shipwreck, a night and a day have I been in the deep;
2CO 11:26 26 in journeyings often, in perils of rivers, in perils of robbers, in perils from my countrymen, in perils from the Gentiles, in perils in the city, in perils in the wilderness, in perils in the sea, in perils among false brethren;
2CO 11:27 27 in labor and travail, in watchings often, in hunger and thirst, in fastings often, in cold and nakedness.
2CO 11:28 28 Besides those things that are without, there is that which presseth upon me daily, anxiety for all the churches.
2CO 11:29 29 Who is weak, and I am not weak? who is caused to stumble, and I burn not?
2CO 11:30 30 If I must needs glory, I will glory of the things that concern my weakness.
2CO 11:31 31 The God and Father of the Lord Jesus, he who is blessed for evermore knoweth that I lie not.
2CO 11:32 32 In Damascus the governor under Aretas the king guarded the city of the Damascenes in order to take me:
2CO 11:33 33 and through a window was I let down in a basket by the wall, and escaped his hands.
2CO 12:1 1 I must needs glory, though it is not expedient; but I will come to visions and revelations of the Lord.
2CO 12:2 2 I know a man in Christ, fourteen years ago (whether in the body, I know not; or whether out of the body, I know not; God knoweth), such a one caught up even to the third heaven.
2CO 12:3 3 And I know such a man (whether in the body, or apart from the body, I know not; God knoweth),
2CO 12:4 4 how that he was caught up into Paradise, and heard unspeakable words, which it is not lawful for a man to utter.
2CO 12:5 5 On behalf of such a one will I glory: but on mine own behalf I will not glory, save in my weaknesses.
2CO 12:6 6 For if I should desire to glory, I shall not be foolish; for I shall speak the truth: but I forbear, lest any man should account of me above that which he seeth me to be , or heareth from me.
2CO 12:7 7 And by reason of the exceeding greatness of the revelations, that I should not be exalted overmuch, there was given to me a thorn in the flesh, a messenger of Satan to buffet me, that I should not be exalted overmuch.
2CO 12:8 8 Concerning this thing I besought the Lord thrice, that it might depart from me.
2CO 12:9 9 And he hath said unto me, My grace is sufficient for thee: for my power is made perfect in weakness. Most gladly therefore will I rather glory in my weaknesses, that the power of Christ may rest upon me.
2CO 12:10 10 Wherefore I take pleasure in weaknesses, in injuries, in necessities, in persecutions, in distresses, for Christ’s sake: for when I am weak, then am I strong.
2CO 12:11 11 I am become foolish: ye compelled me; for I ought to have been commended of you: for in nothing was I behind the very chiefest apostles, though I am nothing.
2CO 12:12 12 Truly the signs of an apostle were wrought among you in all patience, by signs and wonders and mighty works.
2CO 12:13 13 For what is there wherein ye were made inferior to the rest of the churches, except it be that I myself was not a burden to you? forgive me this wrong.
2CO 12:14 14 Behold, this is the third time I am ready to come to you; and I will not be a burden to you: for I seek not yours, but you: for the children ought not to lay up for the parents, but the parents for the children.
2CO 12:15 15 And I will most gladly spend and be spent for your souls. If I love you more abundantly, am I loved the less?
2CO 12:16 16 But be it so, I did not myself burden you; but, being crafty, I caught you with guile.
2CO 12:17 17 Did I take advantage of you by any one of them whom I have sent unto you?
2CO 12:18 18 I exhorted Titus, and I sent the brother with him. Did Titus take any advantage of you? walked we not in the same spirit? walked we not in the same steps?
2CO 12:19 19 Ye think all this time that we are excusing ourselves unto you. In the sight of God speak we in Christ. But all things, beloved, are for your edifying.
2CO 12:20 20 For I fear, lest by any means, when I come, I should find you not such as I would, and should myself be found of you such as ye would not; lest by any means there should be strife, jealousy, wraths, factions, backbitings, whisperings, swellings, tumults;
2CO 12:21 21 lest again when I come my God should humble me before you, and I should mourn for many of them that have sinned heretofore, and repented not of the uncleanness and fornication and lasciviousness which they committed.
2CO 13:1 1 This is the third time I am coming to you. At the mouth of two witnesses or three shall every word be established.
2CO 13:2 2 I have said beforehand, and I do say beforehand, as when I was present the second time, so now, being absent, to them that have sinned heretofore, and to all the rest, that, if I come again, I will not spare;
2CO 13:3 3 seeing that ye seek a proof of Christ that speaketh in me; who to you-ward is not weak, but is powerful in you:
2CO 13:4 4 for he was crucified through weakness, yet he liveth through the power of God. For we also are weak in him, but we shall live with him through the power of God toward you.
2CO 13:5 5 Try your own selves, whether ye are in the faith; prove your own selves. Or know ye not as to your own selves, that Jesus Christ is in you? unless indeed ye be reprobate.
2CO 13:6 6 But I hope that ye shall know that we are not reprobate.
2CO 13:7 7 Now we pray to God that ye do no evil; not that we may appear approved, but that ye may do that which is honorable, though we be as reprobate.
2CO 13:8 8 For we can do nothing against the truth, but for the truth.
2CO 13:9 9 For we rejoice, when we are weak, and ye are strong: this we also pray for, even your perfecting.
2CO 13:10 10 For this cause I write these things while absent, that I may not when present deal sharply, according to the authority which the Lord gave me for building up, and not for casting down.
2CO 13:11 11 Finally, brethren, farewell. Be perfected; be comforted; be of the same mind; live in peace: and the God of love and peace shall be with you.
2CO 13:12 12 Salute one another with a holy kiss.
2CO 13:13 13 All the saints salute you.
2CO 13:14 14 The grace of the Lord Jesus Christ, and the love of God, and the communion of the Holy Spirit, be with you all.
GAL 1:1 1 Paul, an apostle (not from men, neither through man, but through Jesus Christ, and God the Father, who raised him from the dead),
GAL 1:2 2 and all the brethren that are with me, unto the churches of Galatia:
GAL 1:3 3 Grace to you and peace from God the Father, and our Lord Jesus Christ,
GAL 1:4 4 who gave himself for our sins, that he might deliver us out of this present evil world, according to the will of our God and Father:
GAL 1:5 5 to whom be the glory for ever and ever. Amen.
GAL 1:6 6 I marvel that ye are so quickly removing from him that called you in the grace of Christ unto a different gospel;
GAL 1:7 7 which is not another gospel : only there are some that trouble you, and would pervert the gospel of Christ.
GAL 1:8 8 But though we, or an angel from heaven, should preach unto you any gospel other than that which we preached unto you, let him be anathema.
GAL 1:9 9 As we have said before, so say I now again, If any man preacheth unto you any gospel other than that which ye received, let him be anathema.
GAL 1:10 10 For am I now seeking the favor of men, or of God? or am I striving to please men? if I were still pleasing men, I should not be a servant of Christ.
GAL 1:11 11 For I make known to you, brethren, as touching the gospel which was preached by me, that it is not after man.
GAL 1:12 12 For neither did I receive it from man, nor was I taught it, but it came to me through revelation of Jesus Christ.
GAL 1:13 13 For ye have heard of my manner of life in time past in the Jews’ religion, how that beyond measure I persecuted the church of God, and made havoc of it:
GAL 1:14 14 and I advanced in the Jews’ religion beyond many of mine own age among my countrymen, being more exceedingly zealous for the traditions of my fathers.
GAL 1:15 15 But when it was the good pleasure of God, who separated me, even from my mother’s womb, and called me through his grace,
GAL 1:16 16 to reveal his Son in me, that I might preach him among the Gentiles; straightway I conferred not with flesh and blood:
GAL 1:17 17 neither went I up to Jerusalem to them that were apostles before me: but I went away into Arabia; and again I returned unto Damascus.
GAL 1:18 18 Then after three years I went up to Jerusalem to visit Cephas, and tarried with him fifteen days.
GAL 1:19 19 But other of the apostles saw I none, save James the Lord’s brother.
GAL 1:20 20 Now touching the things which I write unto you, behold, before God, I lie not.
GAL 1:21 21 Then I came into the regions of Syria and Cilicia.
GAL 1:22 22 And I was still unknown by face unto the churches of Judæa which were in Christ:
GAL 1:23 23 but they only heard say, He that once persecuted us now preacheth the faith of which he once made havoc;
GAL 1:24 24 and they glorified God in me.
GAL 2:1 1 Then after the space of fourteen years I went up again to Jerusalem with Barnabas, taking Titus also with me.
GAL 2:2 2 And I went up by revelation; and I laid before them the gospel which I preach among the Gentiles but privately before them who were of repute, lest by any means I should be running, or had run, in vain.
GAL 2:3 3 But not even Titus who was with me, being a Greek, was compelled to be circumcised:
GAL 2:4 4 and that because of the false brethren privily brought in, who came in privily to spy out our liberty which we have in Christ Jesus, that they might bring us into bondage:
GAL 2:5 5 to whom we gave place in the way of subjection, no, not for an hour; that the truth of the gospel might continue with you.
GAL 2:6 6 But from those who were reputed to be somewhat (whatsoever they were, it maketh no matter to me: God accepteth not man’s person)—they, I say, who were of repute imparted nothing to me:
GAL 2:7 7 but contrariwise, when they saw that I had been intrusted with the gospel of the uncircumcision, even as Peter with the gospel of the circumcision
GAL 2:8 8 (for he that wrought for Peter unto the apostleship of the circumcision wrought for me also unto the Gentiles);
GAL 2:9 9 and when they perceived the grace that was given unto me, James and Cephas and John, they who were reputed to be pillars, gave to me and Barnabas the right hands of fellowship, that we should go unto the Gentiles, and they unto the circumcision;
GAL 2:10 10 only they would that we should remember the poor; which very thing I was also zealous to do.
GAL 2:11 11 But when Cephas came to Antioch, I resisted him to the face, because he stood condemned.
GAL 2:12 12 For before that certain came from James, he ate with the Gentiles; but when they came, he drew back and separated himself, fearing them that were of the circumcision.
GAL 2:13 13 And the rest of the Jews dissembled likewise with him; insomuch that even Barnabas was carried away with their dissimulation.
GAL 2:14 14 But when I saw that they walked not uprightly according to the truth of the gospel, I said unto Cephas before them all, If thou, being a Jew, livest as do the Gentiles, and not as do the Jews, how compellest thou the Gentiles to live as do the Jews?
GAL 2:15 15 We being Jews by nature, and not sinners of the Gentiles,
GAL 2:16 16 yet knowing that a man is not justified by the works of the law but through faith in Jesus Christ, even we believed on Christ Jesus, that we might be justified by faith in Christ, and not by the works of the law: because by the works of the law shall no flesh be justified.
GAL 2:17 17 But if, while we sought to be justified in Christ, we ourselves also were found sinners, is Christ a minister of sin? God forbid.
GAL 2:18 18 For if I build up again those things which I destroyed, I prove myself a transgressor.
GAL 2:19 19 For I through the law died unto the law, that I might live unto God.
GAL 2:20 20 I have been crucified with Christ; and it is no longer I that live, but Christ liveth in me: and that life which I now live in the flesh I live in faith, the faith which is in the Son of God, who loved me, and gave himself up for me.
GAL 2:21 21 I do not make void the grace of God: for if righteousness is through the law, then Christ died for nought.
GAL 3:1 1 O foolish Galatians, who did bewitch you, before whose eyes Jesus Christ was openly set forth crucified?
GAL 3:2 2 This only would I learn from you, Received ye the Spirit by the works of the law, or by the hearing of faith?
GAL 3:3 3 Are ye so foolish? having begun in the Spirit, are ye now perfected in the flesh?
GAL 3:4 4 Did ye suffer so many things in vain? if it be indeed in vain.
GAL 3:5 5 He therefore that supplieth to you the Spirit, and worketh miracles among you, doeth he it by the works of the law, or by the hearing of faith?
GAL 3:6 6 Even as Abraham believed God, and it was reckoned unto him for righteousness.
GAL 3:7 7 Know therefore that they that are of faith, the same are sons of Abraham.
GAL 3:8 8 And the scripture, foreseeing that God would justify the Gentiles by faith, preached the gospel beforehand unto Abraham, saying , In thee shall all the nations be blessed.
GAL 3:9 9 So then they that are of faith are blessed with the faithful Abraham.
